It does not attempt to redesign the entire security model.\n\n## Context\n\nFP-MCP-147 introduced `forgepilot_execute_terminal_command` in the MCP bridge.\n\nThe tool works and has been verified live, but first use revealed practical hardening needs:\n\n1. terminal commands run as the normal `ridasaidd` user,\n2. the server is behind Tailnet and proxy boundaries, but the MCP bridge process listens broadly,\n3. the terminal tool currently has no terminal-specific kill switch,\n4. the sanitized PATH does not reliably resolve Node or pnpm,\n5. command policy should block container-engine commands because the user is in the docker group,\n6. evidence creation is self-observing and needs a later batching/finalization rule.\n\n## Scope\n\nImplement only the following bridge-level changes:\n\n1. Add terminal-specific disable switch:\n   - environment variable: `FORGEPILOT_TERMINAL_EXECUTION_ENABLED`\n   - when set to `false`, `forgepilot_execute_terminal_command` must refuse execution before running any shell command.\n\n2. Improve sanitized PATH:\n   - ensure `/home/ridasaidd/.nvm/versions/node/v24.4.1/bin` is present in the command PATH,\n   - `pnpm build` should work without manually prefixing PATH.\n\n3. Block container-engine commands:\n   - docker,\n   - podman,\n   - nerdctl.\n\n4. Preserve existing behavior:\n   - MCP approval popup remains the approval surface,\n   - workspace allowlist remains enforced,\n   - existing hard blocks remain enforced,\n   - stdout/stderr/evidence behavior remains intact,\n   - runner start behavior is not weakened.\n\n## Non-goals\n\n- Do not change Auth0 behavior.\n- Do not change Tailscale ACLs from code.\n- Do not require a new Unix user in this packet.\n- Do not redesign evidence batching in this packet.\n- Do not weaken the MCP approval model.\n- Do not expose new command execution surfaces.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n1. `pnpm build` passes in the MCP bridge repo.\n2. `forgepilot_execute_terminal_command` remains exposed after service restart.\n3. With terminal execution enabled or unset, a read-only command still works.\n4. With `FORGEPILOT_TERMINAL_EXECUTION_ENABLED=false`, terminal command execution is refused.\n5. `pnpm build` works through the terminal tool without a manual PATH prefix.\n6. Container-engine commands are blocked and recorded as blocked evidence.\n7. Existing behavior for workspace allowlist and command evidence is preserved.\n8. Implementation summary and verification evidence are recorded under `runs/FP-MCP-148/`.\n\n## Audit Notes\n\nThis packet should be audited after implementation. ForgePilot must distinguish availability from permission and permission from trust.\n\n## Scope\n\nAdd:\n\n```text\ndocs/model-roster-and-profiling-rules.md\n```\n\nUpdate:\n\n```text\ndocs/forgepilot-progress-checklist.md\ninstrument-projects/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp/checklists/server-shadow-refactor.md\n```\n\n## Required Rules\n\nThe document must define:\n\n- model roster purpose,\n- model identity fields,\n- allowed model roles,\n- allowed task classes,\n- trust states,\n- promotion criteria,\n- demotion criteria,\n- evidence requirements,\n- default restrictions for unobserved models,\n- MCP bridge shadow-refactor participation boundary.\n\n## Required Boundary\n\nModel availability is not trust.\n\nA model may be allowed for read-only planning before it is allowed for implementation.\n\nNo model may modify project code unless a scoped packet explicitly grants implementation authority.\n\n## Safety Requirements\n\nThis packet must not execute models, call OpenCode, refactor the MCP bridge, add MCP tools, change OpenAI Actions schema, create SQLite schema, implement routing, rank models globally, rename historical packets, move historical packets, or delete historical packets.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\nThis packet does not select a winning model, implement routing, score models, implement model automation, run benchmarks, or create an OpenCode plugin.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n1. Model roster and profiling rules document exists.\n2. Allowed roles and task classes are defined.\n3. Trust states are defined.\n4. Promotion and demotion criteria are defined.\n5. Initial known OpenCode Go model roster is recorded as available but not automatically trusted.\n6. MCP bridge shadow-refactor model participation boundary is defined.\n7. Checklist references FP-META-029 completion.\n8. No implementation code changes are made.\n9. Final git status is clean after evidence is recorded.\nEOF\n\ngit add packets/FP-META-029.md\ngit commit -m \"Add FP-META-029 model roster rules packet\"\ngit log --oneline -5\ngit status --short",
runs/FP-META-029/terminal-commands/TERM-20260702T104124783Z-7b933a3c-result.json:8:  "command": "mkdir -p runs/FP-META-029\ncat > docs/model-roster-and-profiling-rules.md <<'EOF'\n# Model Roster and Profiling Rules\n\n## Purpose\n\nDefine how ForgePilot records model availability, allowed roles, task classes, trust states, promotion, and demotion.\n\nForgePilot must not treat a model as trusted merely because it is available through OpenCode Go or any other provider.\n\n## Core Boundary\n\n```text\nModel availability is not trust.\n\nPermission to plan is not permission to implement.\n\nNo model may modify project code unless a scoped packet explicitly grants implementation authority.\n```\n\n## Model Identity Fields\n\nA model roster entry should include:\n\n| Field | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| Model ID | Stable model name used in packets and artifacts. |\n| Provider/source | Where the model is accessed. |\n| Availability state | Whether the model is available to the operator. |\n| Allowed roles | Roles the model may currently perform. |\n| Allowed task classes | Task classes the model may currently attempt. |\n| Trust state | Current ForgePilot trust classification for this model. |\n| Evidence basis | Committed evidence justifying the trust state. |\n| Promotion criteria | What evidence would allow broader use. |\n| Demotion criteria | What evidence would restrict or remove use. |\n| Notes | Operational notes, caveats, cost/limit context if relevant. |\n\n## Allowed Model Roles\n\n| Role | Meaning | Code modification allowed? |\n|---|---|---|\n| `STRUCTURE_MAPPER` | Read code/artifacts and describe structure. | No |\n| `RISK_ANALYST` | Identify risk zones, boundaries, and failure modes. | No |\n| `PLANNER` | Propose scoped plans and packet sequences. | No |\n| `PLAN_AUDITOR` | Audit plans for scope, safety, and evidence gaps. | No |\n| `FIXTURE_DESIGNER` | Propose baseline fixtures or comparison checks. | No |\n| `IMPLEMENTATION_CANDIDATE` | Modify code within a scoped packet. | Yes, only if explicitly granted |\n| `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDITOR` | Audit code changes and evidence. | No |\n| `COMPARATOR` | Compare model outputs or implementation candidates. | No |\n\n## Allowed Task Classes\n\n| Task class | Meaning | Default permission |\n|---|---|---|\n| `READ_ONLY_STRUCTURE_MAP` | Map files, modules, and responsibilities. | Allowed for unobserved models in bounded packets |\n| `RISK_IDENTIFICATION` | Identify risk zones and guardrails. | Allowed for unobserved models in bounded packets |\n| `REFACTOR_PLAN` | Propose a plan without edits. | Allowed for observed planning models |\n| `PLAN_AUDIT` | Audit a plan without edits. | Allowed for observed planning/audit models |\n| `FIXTURE_SUGGESTION` | Suggest tests/snapshots/fixtures. | Allowed for observed planning/audit models |\n| `SMALL_MECHANICAL_CHANGE` | Make one scoped mechanical code change. | Requires promotion |\n| `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT` | Audit a code change. | Requires observed audit usefulness |\n| `COMPARISON` | Compare outputs/candidates. | Requires observed comparison usefulness |\n\n## Trust States\n\n| Trust state | Meaning | Default permissions |\n|---|---|---|\n| `UNOBSERVED` | No ForgePilot evidence yet. | Read-only structure/risk tasks only. |\n| `OBSERVED_PLANNING_ONLY` | Has produced useful planning/analysis evidence. | Planning and read-only analysis. |\n| `OBSERVED_AUDIT_USEFUL` | Has produced useful audits. | Plan/code audit, no code edits. |\n| `OBSERVED_SAFE_SMALL_CHANGE` | Has successfully completed small scoped implementation with evidence. | May be considered for small mechanical changes. |\n| `PROMOTED_FOR_EXECUTION` | Sufficient evidence supports implementation use in scoped packets. | Scoped implementation allowed. |\n| `DEMOTED` | Evidence shows unreliable, unsafe, or unsuitable behavior. | Restricted or disallowed. |\n\n## Promotion Criteria\n\nA model may be promoted only through committed evidence.\n\nPromotion may consider:\n\n- completed packet outputs,\n- verification results,\n- audit acceptance,\n- adherence to scope,\n- absence of forbidden edits,\n- quality of reasoning artifacts,\n- ability to preserve boundaries,\n- consistency across multiple tasks.\n\nPromotion must not be based only on reputation, benchmark marketing, or one successful informal conversation.\n\n## Demotion Criteria\n\nA model should be demoted if evidence shows:\n\n- unauthorized file edits,\n- scope expansion,\n- invented evidence,\n- tool contract changes outside scope,\n- unsafe recommendations,\n- repeated hallucinated paths or commands,\n- poor audit quality,\n- failure to preserve explicit boundaries,\n- implementation that passes superficially but violates packet intent.\n\n## Evidence Requirements\n\nEvery model trust change must cite committed evidence.\n\nEvidence may include:\n\n- model output artifacts,\n- verification artifacts,\n- audit artifacts,\n- comparison artifacts,\n- checklist updates that point to evidence,\n- packet completion records.\n\nA checklist entry alone is not evidence unless it points to committed artifacts.\n\n## Initial Known OpenCode Go Model Roster\n\nThe following models are recorded as known available candidates from the user's OpenCode Go context.\n\nAvailability does not imply trust.\n\n| Model ID | Initial trust state | Initial allowed roles | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `GLM-5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `GLM-5.1` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `Kimi K2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `Kimi K2.6` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiMo-V2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiMo-V2.5-Pro` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiniMax M2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiniMax M2.7` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiniMax M3` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `Qwen3.6 Plus` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only until observed. |\n| `Qwen3.7 Plus` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only until observed. |\n| `Qwen3.7 Max` | `OBSERVED_AUDIT_USEFUL` | `PLAN_AUDITOR`, `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDITOR`, `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Prior ForgePilot audit evidence exists, but not blanket implementation authority. |\n| `DeepSeek V4 Pro` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Distinct from high variant unless evidence says otherwise. |\n| `DeepSeek V4 Pro High` | `OBSERVED_SAFE_SMALL_CHANGE` | `PLANNER`, `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST`, `IMPLEMENTATION_CANDIDATE` | Prior ForgePilot implementation evidence exists, but only scoped packet authority may permit edits. |\n\n## Default Restrictions for Unobserved Models\n\nUnobserved models may only perform:\n\n- structure mapping,\n- risk identification,\n- read-only summarization,\n- plan critique without authority.\n\nUnobserved models must not:\n\n- edit code,\n- generate patches intended for direct application,\n- change tool contracts,\n- make admission/routing decisions,\n- be treated as authoritative auditors,\n- receive broad refactor tasks.\n\n## MCP Bridge Shadow-Refactor Boundary\n\nFor the MCP bridge shadow refactor:\n\n- models may participate in read-only planning and audit packets first,\n- no model may edit `/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp` until a project-specific packet grants implementation authority,\n- CRITICAL zones remain protected regardless of model trust state,\n- DeepSeek V4 Pro High may be considered for the first small mechanical implementation only after baseline verification and packet authorization,\n- Qwen3.7 Max may be considered for audit roles,\n- unobserved models may be used for independent read-only structure or risk analysis tasks.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\nThese rules do not implement routing, scoring, global ranking, model automation, OpenCode plugins, or model execution.\n\n## Boundary\n\nThis document defines model participation rules.\n\nIt does not run models, select a winner, authorize implementation, or change any project source code.\nEOF\npython - worker execution out of ad hoc terminal commands and into a ForgePilot-controlled lifecycle with explicit request, start, status, result, and evidence artifacts.\n\nFP-MCP-153 should make GPT able to start a bounded OpenCode worker safely while preserving ForgePilot's evidence discipline.\n\n## Background\n\nFP-MCP-147 gave GPT controlled terminal hands.\n\nFP-MCP-148 hardened terminal execution with a kill switch and Node tooling path support.\n\nFP-MCP-149 inventoried local OpenCode telemetry metadata before changing the legacy OpenCode server state.\n\nFP-MCP-150 created the ForgePilot master plan.\n\nFP-MCP-151 added terminal rate limiting and command shaping, including refusal of direct `opencode run` worker execution through the terminal tool.\n\nFP-MCP-152 added a first-class workspace snapshot capability.\n\nThe next gap is controlled OpenCode worker invocation.\n\n## Scope\n\nImplement OpenCode worker invocation v1 in:\n\n`/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp`\n\nThe implementation should add dedicated MCP tools and supporting module code.\n\n## Required Tools\n\nAdd GPT-facing MCP tools:\n\n1. `forgepilot_start_opencode_worker`\n2. `forgepilot_get_opencode_worker_status`\n3. `forgepilot_read_opencode_worker_result`\n\n## Tool 1: `forgepilot_start_opencode_worker`\n\n### Required Inputs\n\n- `packetId: string`\n- `requestId?: string`\n- `targetWorkspaceId: string`\n- `promptFile: string`\n- `title?: string`\n- `modelId?: string`\n- `timeoutSeconds?: number`\n- `approval: \"START_OPENCODE_WORKER\"`\n\n### Requirements\n\nThe tool must:\n\n- require exact approval `START_OPENCODE_WORKER`,\n- allow only known workspaces,\n- require `promptFile` to be inside the ForgePilot repository or allowed workspace,\n- refuse if the prompt file is missing,\n- refuse if another worker for the same packet is already active,\n- start a fresh `opencode run` process without attaching to the legacy OpenCode server,\n- write worker evidence under `runs/<packetId>/opencode-workers/<workerId>/`,\n- return immediately after the worker has been started,\n- not wait for model completion,\n- not stop or restart the legacy OpenCode server.\n\n### Required Start Evidence\n\nThe worker directory must contain at minimum:\n\n- `start-request.json`,\n- `pid.txt`,\n- `stdout.log`,\n- `stderr.log`,\n- `status.json`.\n\n`status.json` must initially record:\n\n- worker id,\n- packet id,\n- request id if provided,\n- model id if provided,\n- target workspace,\n- prompt file,\n- process id,\n- started timestamp,\n- status `RUNNING`,\n- stdout/stderr paths,\n- approval source.\n\n## Tool 2: `forgepilot_get_opencode_worker_status`\n\n### Required Inputs\n\n- `packetId: string`\n- `workerId: string`\n\n### Requirements\n\nThe tool must:\n\n- read the worker status artifact,\n- check whether the process is still active,\n- update status to `SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`, or `UNKNOWN_EXIT` if the process has ended,\n- preserve stdout/stderr paths,\n- return structured status,\n- not start any process.\n\n## Tool 3: `forgepilot_read_opencode_worker_result`\n\n### Required Inputs\n\n- `packetId: string`\n- `workerId: string`\n- `maxChars?: number`\n\n### Requirements\n\nThe tool must:\n\n- read status, stdout, and stderr for an existing worker,\n- return bounded stdout/stderr content,\n- report truncation,\n- include evidence paths,\n- not start, stop, or restart any process.\n\n## Safety Requirements\n\nThe implementation must not:\n\n- attach to the running legacy OpenCode server,\n- stop or restart the legacy OpenCode server,\n- read raw secrets,\n- expose environment variable values,\n- support arbitrary shell commands from user input,\n- support arbitrary process killing,\n- run more than one active worker per packet in v1.\n\nThe implementation should construct the `opencode run` invocation using fixed arguments, not arbitrary shell strings.\n\n## Environment Requirements\n\nUse the Node tooling path already established in FP-MCP-148:\n\n`/home/ridasaidd/.nvm/versions/node/v24.4.1/bin`\n\nUse a sanitized environment.\n\nDo not print or record secret environment values.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\nThis packet does not implement:\n\n- full OpenCode telemetry ingestion,\n- token/cost/session database parsing,\n- model performance admission,\n- SQLite persistence,\n- routing decisions,\n- multi-worker scheduling,\n- worker cancellation,\n- background queue management,\n- prompt generation,\n- MCP file refactor beyond what is needed for v1.\n\n## Refactor Note\n\nThe MCP bridge has grown large. Once worker invocation and telemetry capture are stable enough, create a future refactor packet to split bridge logic into smaller modules.\n\nDo not perform broad refactoring in FP-MCP-153 unless required for the worker tool implementation.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n1. MCP bridge build passes.\n2. The three worker tools are visible through MCP discovery after refresh/reconnect.\n3. Invalid approval for start is rejected.\n4. Missing prompt file is rejected.\n5. Starting a worker creates a worker directory and required artifacts.\n6. Start returns without waiting for model completion.\n7. Status tool returns `RUNNING` while active or terminal status after completion.\n8. Result tool returns bounded stdout/stderr and truncation metadata.\n9. Existing terminal tool still refuses direct `opencode run`.\n10. Legacy OpenCode server remains running and is not attached to, stopped, or restarted.\n11. ForgePilot and bridge repositories are clean after implementation evidence is recorded.\n\n## Verification Requirements\n\nVerify at minimum:\n\n- `pnpm build` in bridge repo,\n- tool discovery includes the three tools,\n- invalid approval rejection,\n- missing prompt rejection,\n- one bounded worker start using an existing prompt file,\n- worker status read,\n- worker result read,\n- legacy OpenCode server process remains present,\n- direct terminal `opencode run` remains refused,\n- implementation evidence recorded under `runs/FP-MCP-153/`.\n\n## Expected Result\n\nGPT can start and observe bounded OpenCode worker runs through purpose-built MCP tools rather than generic terminal supervision.\n\nFP-MCP-153 prepares the system for FP-MCP-154 OpenCode Telemetry Capture v1.\nEOF\n\ngit add packets/FP-MCP-153.md\ngit commit -m \"Add FP-MCP-153 OpenCode worker invocation packet\"\ngit push\ngit status --short",

runs/FP-META-029\ncat > docs/model-roster-and-profiling-rules.md <<'EOF'\n# Model Roster and Profiling Rules\n\n## Purpose\n\nDefine how ForgePilot records model availability, allowed roles, task classes, trust states, promotion, and demotion.\n\nForgePilot must not treat a model as trusted merely because it is available through OpenCode Go or any other provider.\n\n## Core Boundary\n\n```text\nModel availability is not trust.\n\nPermission to plan is not permission to implement.\n\nNo model may modify project code unless a scoped packet explicitly grants implementation authority.\n```\n\n## Model Identity Fields\n\nA model roster entry should include:\n\n| Field | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| Model ID | Stable model name used in packets and artifacts. |\n| Provider/source | Where the model is accessed. |\n| Availability state | Whether the model is available to the operator. |\n| Allowed roles | Roles the model may currently perform. |\n| Allowed task classes | Task classes the model may currently attempt. |\n| Trust state | Current ForgePilot trust classification for this model. |\n| Evidence basis | Committed evidence justifying the trust state. |\n| Promotion criteria | What evidence would allow broader use. |\n| Demotion criteria | What evidence would restrict or remove use. |\n| Notes | Operational notes, caveats, cost/limit context if relevant. |\n\n## Allowed Model Roles\n\n| Role | Meaning | Code modification allowed? |\n|---|---|---|\n| `STRUCTURE_MAPPER` | Read code/artifacts and describe structure. | No |\n| `RISK_ANALYST` | Identify risk zones, boundaries, and failure modes. | No |\n| `PLANNER` | Propose scoped plans and packet sequences. | No |\n| `PLAN_AUDITOR` | Audit plans for scope, safety, and evidence gaps. | No |\n| `FIXTURE_DESIGNER` | Propose baseline fixtures or comparison checks. | No |\n| `IMPLEMENTATION_CANDIDATE` | Modify code within a scoped packet. | Yes, only if explicitly granted |\n| `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDITOR` | Audit code changes and evidence. | No |\n| `COMPARATOR` | Compare model outputs or implementation candidates. | No |\n\n## Allowed Task Classes\n\n| Task class | Meaning | Default permission |\n|---|---|---|\n| `READ_ONLY_STRUCTURE_MAP` | Map files, modules, and responsibilities. | Allowed for unobserved models in bounded packets |\n| `RISK_IDENTIFICATION` | Identify risk zones and guardrails. | Allowed for unobserved models in bounded packets |\n| `REFACTOR_PLAN` | Propose a plan without edits. | Allowed for observed planning models |\n| `PLAN_AUDIT` | Audit a plan without edits. | Allowed for observed planning/audit models |\n| `FIXTURE_SUGGESTION` | Suggest tests/snapshots/fixtures. | Allowed for observed planning/audit models |\n| `SMALL_MECHANICAL_CHANGE` | Make one scoped mechanical code change. | Requires promotion |\n| `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT` | Audit a code change. | Requires observed audit usefulness |\n| `COMPARISON` | Compare outputs/candidates. | Requires observed comparison usefulness |\n\n## Trust States\n\n| Trust state | Meaning | Default permissions |\n|---|---|---|\n| `UNOBSERVED` | No ForgePilot evidence yet. | Read-only structure/risk tasks only. |\n| `OBSERVED_PLANNING_ONLY` | Has produced useful planning/analysis evidence. | Planning and read-only analysis. |\n| `OBSERVED_AUDIT_USEFUL` | Has produced useful audits. | Plan/code audit, no code edits. |\n| `OBSERVED_SAFE_SMALL_CHANGE` | Has successfully completed small scoped implementation with evidence. | May be considered for small mechanical changes. |\n| `PROMOTED_FOR_EXECUTION` | Sufficient evidence supports implementation use in scoped packets. | Scoped implementation allowed. |\n| `DEMOTED` | Evidence shows unreliable, unsafe, or unsuitable behavior. | Restricted or disallowed. |\n\n## Promotion Criteria\n\nA model may be promoted only through committed evidence.\n\nPromotion may consider:\n\n- completed packet outputs,\n- verification results,\n- audit acceptance,\n- adherence to scope,\n- absence of forbidden edits,\n- quality of reasoning artifacts,\n- ability to preserve boundaries,\n- consistency across multiple tasks.\n\nPromotion must not be based only on reputation, benchmark marketing, or one successful informal conversation.\n\n## Demotion Criteria\n\nA model should be demoted if evidence shows:\n\n- unauthorized file edits,\n- scope expansion,\n- invented evidence,\n- tool contract changes outside scope,\n- unsafe recommendations,\n- repeated hallucinated paths or commands,\n- poor audit quality,\n- failure to preserve explicit boundaries,\n- implementation that passes superficially but violates packet intent.\n\n## Evidence Requirements\n\nEvery model trust change must cite committed evidence.\n\nEvidence may include:\n\n- model output artifacts,\n- verification artifacts,\n- audit artifacts,\n- comparison artifacts,\n- checklist updates that point to evidence,\n- packet completion records.\n\nA checklist entry alone is not evidence unless it points to committed artifacts.\n\n## Initial Known OpenCode Go Model Roster\n\nThe following models are recorded as known available candidates from the user's OpenCode Go context.\n\nAvailability does not imply trust.\n\n| Model ID | Initial trust state | Initial allowed roles | Notes |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `GLM-5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `GLM-5.1` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `Kimi K2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `Kimi K2.6` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiMo-V2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiMo-V2.5-Pro` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiniMax M2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiniMax M2.7` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `MiniMax M3` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |\n| `Qwen3.6 Plus` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only until observed. |\n| `Qwen3.7 Plus` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only until observed. |\n| `Qwen3.7 Max` | `OBSERVED_AUDIT_USEFUL` | `PLAN_AUDITOR`, `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDITOR`, `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Prior ForgePilot audit evidence exists, but not blanket implementation authority. |\n| `DeepSeek V4 Pro` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Distinct from high variant unless evidence says otherwise. |\n| `DeepSeek V4 Pro High` | `OBSERVED_SAFE_SMALL_CHANGE` | `PLANNER`, `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST`, `IMPLEMENTATION_CANDIDATE` | Prior ForgePilot implementation evidence exists, but only scoped packet authority may permit edits. |\n\n## Default Restrictions for Unobserved Models\n\nUnobserved models may only perform:\n\n- structure mapping,\n- risk identification,\n- read-only summarization,\n- plan critique without authority.\n\nUnobserved models must not:\n\n- edit code,\n- generate patches intended for direct application,\n- change tool contracts,\n- make admission/routing decisions,\n- be treated as authoritative auditors,\n- receive broad refactor tasks.\n\n## MCP Bridge Shadow-Refactor Boundary\n\nFor the MCP bridge shadow refactor:\n\n- models may participate in read-only planning and audit packets first,\n- no model may edit `/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp` until a project-specific packet grants implementation authority,\n- CRITICAL zones remain protected regardless of model trust state,\n- DeepSeek V4 Pro High may be considered for the first small mechanical implementation only after baseline verification and packet authorization,\n- Qwen3.7 Max may be considered for audit roles,\n- unobserved models may be used for independent read-only structure or risk analysis tasks.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\nThese rules do not implement routing, scoring, global ranking, model automation, OpenCode plugins, or model execution.\n\n## Boundary\n\nThis document defines model participation rules.\n\nIt does not run models, select a winner, authorize implementation, or change any project source code.\nEOF\npython - <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np = Path('docs/forgepilot-progress-checklist.md')\ns = p.read_text()\nrow = '| Model roster and profiling rules | `COMPLETE` | docs/invalidation-propagation-rules.md <<'EOF'\n# Invalidation Propagation Rules\n\n## Purpose\n\nDefine how ForgePilot treats downstream artifacts when admitted evidence is later invalidated, quarantined, or otherwise defeated.\n\n## Core Rule\n\n```text\nInvalidation must not only revoke evidence authority.\nIt must also identify downstream artifacts whose claims may now be stale.\n```\n\nA revocation mechanism that only logs invalidation but leaves dependent reports unmarked is incomplete.\n\n## Relationship to Evidence Root Boundary\n\nFP-META-030 defines evidence-root and workspace boundaries.\n\nInvalidation propagation follows those boundaries:\n\n- core evidence invalidation affects core downstream artifacts that explicitly depended on that evidence,\n- project workspace invalidation affects project workspace downstream artifacts that explicitly depended on that evidence,\n- cross-workspace propagation occurs only through explicit dependency edges,\n- artifacts are not rewritten silently across boundaries.\n\n## Terms\n\n| Term | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| Invalidation source | The evidence record, admission event, validation result, review request, or invalidation event that revokes or defeats prior authority. |\n| Dependency edge | An explicit recorded relationship showing that one artifact relied on another artifact or evidence record. |\n| Downstream artifact | Any report, summary, comparison, admission decision, checklist update, model trust assignment, or derived record that depends on earlier evidence. |\n| Stale report | A report whose input evidence changed or was invalidated after the report was produced. |\n| Affected artifact | A downstream artifact that may have depended on invalidated evidence. |\n| Requires reconstruction | A derived artifact whose current claims must be regenerated or re-evaluated before use. |\n\n## Required Downstream States\n\nFuture propagation-capable reports or ledgers should be able to express:\n\n| State | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `NOT_AFFECTED` | No explicit dependency on invalidated evidence is known. |\n| `AFFECTED_BY_INVALIDATION` | Artifact has an explicit dependency on invalidated/quarantined evidence. |\n| `STALE_REPORT` | Report was generated before an invalidation that affects one or more inputs. |\n| `REQUIRES_RECONSTRUCTION` | Artifact must be regenerated or re-evaluated before it can be used as current evidence. |\n| `SUPERSEDED_BY_RECONSTRUCTION` | Artifact has been replaced by a later reconstruction that accounts for invalidation. |\n| `UNKNOWN_DEPENDENCY_STATUS` | Dependency edges are insufficient to determine impact. |\n\n## Propagation Semantics\n\nWhen evidence is invalidated or quarantined:\n\n1. The invalidation event defeats the authority of the targeted admission where current admission logic says it does.\n2. ForgePilot must identify artifacts with explicit dependency edges to the invalidated evidence.\n3. Dependent reports should be marked `STALE_REPORT` or `AFFECTED_BY_INVALIDATION`.\n4. Derived artifacts that may be reused for future decisions should be marked `REQUIRES_RECONSTRUCTION`.\n5. New reports must account for the invalidation before claiming current status.\n6. Historical reports must not be edited in place; later artifacts must record that they were affected or superseded.\n\n## Cross-Workspace Propagation\n\nInvalidation does not cross workspace boundaries by default.\n\nPropagation from core to project workspace, project workspace to core, or project workspace to project workspace requires explicit dependency edges.\n\nExamples:\n\n| Scenario | Propagation |\n|---|---|\n| Core report depends only on core packets/runs | Core invalidation may affect it. |\n| Instrument-project report depends only on instrument workspace evidence | Instrument invalidation may affect it. |\n| Core V1 report explicitly includes instrument workspace evidence | Relevant instrument invalidation may affect the core report. |\n| Instrument workspace file merely exists under bootstrap internal path | No propagation unless explicitly referenced. |\n\n## Non-Propagation Cases\n\nInvalidation must not automatically affect:\n\n- artifacts with no explicit dependency edge,\n- reports produced from a different evidence root,\n- project workspaces not selected as inputs,\n- historical artifacts that merely coexist in the repository,\n- checklist rows that do not cite the invalidated evidence.\n\nIf dependency status is unclear, the correct state is:\n\n```text\nUNKNOWN_DEPENDENCY_STATUS\n```\n\nnot `NOT_AFFECTED`.\n\n## Append-Only Requirement\n\nPropagation must be append-only.\n\nForgePilot must not rewrite old reports to erase prior claims.\n\nInstead, it should append or generate later artifacts that say:\n\n```text\nThis artifact was affected by invalidation event X.\nThis artifact requires reconstruction.\nThis artifact was superseded by reconstruction Y.\n```\n\n## Model Trust Assignments\n\nModel trust assignments are downstream artifacts when they rely on prior model performance evidence.\n\nA model promotion or demotion must be append-only and provenance-bearing.\n\nIf a trust assignment is later shown to be wrong, the correction should record:\n\n- prior assignment,\n- invalidating evidence,\n- assigned_by or corrected_by actor,\n- assignment method,\n- affected task class,\n- affected project/context,\n- new trust state,\n- evidence references.\n\nA trust label must not be silently overwritten.\n\n## Current Implementation Status\n\n```text\nPROPAGATION_RULE_DEFINED_IMPLEMENTATION_NOT_PRESENT\n```\n\nCurrent code records admission invalidation events and derives eligibility using defeating invalidation events.\n\nCurrent source search did not find implemented downstream states such as:\n\n```text\nAFFECTED_BY_INVALIDATION\nSTALE_REPORT\nREQUIRES_RECONSTRUCTION\nSUPERSEDED_BY_RECONSTRUCTION\nUNKNOWN_DEPENDENCY_STATUS\n```\n\nTherefore FP-META-031 defines the required semantics but does not claim current implementation conformance.\n\n## Evidence Quality\n\nThe current implementation status is based on command-captured source/docs search in:\n\n```text\nruns/FP-META-031/invalidation-source-search.txt\n```\n\nTrust classification:\n\n```text\nTIER_1_STATIC_SOURCE_INSPECTION\n```\n\nThis is stronger than narrative self-report because search output and source excerpts are preserved. It is weaker than runtime tracing or tests proving propagation behavior.\n\n## Future Implementation Requirements\n\nA future implementation packet should define or implement:\n\n- dependency edge capture,\n- stale report markers,\n- affected artifact markers,\n- reconstruction requirement markers,\n- supersession markers,\n- report generation that checks invalidation events,\n- tests proving invalidation affects downstream report status.\n\n## Boundary\n\nFP-META-031 defines invalidation propagation semantics only.\n\nIt does not implement propagation, change schema, mutate historical reports, or mark existing artifacts stale.\nEOF\npython - <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np = Path('docs/forgepilot-progress-checklist.md')\ns = p.read_text()\nrow = '| Invalidation propagation rules | `COMPLETE` | `FP-META-031` | Downstream invalidation semantics are documented in `docs/invalidation-propagation-rules.md`; current implementation status is `PROPAGATION_RULE_DEFINED_IMPLEMENTATION_NOT_PRESENT`. | Governance only; no propagation implementation yet. |'\nmarker = '| Evidence root and workspace boundary | `COMPLETE` | `FP-META-030` | Boundary rules are documented in `docs/evidence-root-and-workspace-boundary.md`; It is weaker than runtime tracing or tests proving propagation behavior.\n\n## Future Implementation Requirements\n\nA future implementation packet should define or implement:\n\n- dependency edge capture,\n- stale report markers,\n- affected artifact markers,\n- reconstruction requirement markers,\n- supersession markers,\n- report generation that checks invalidation events,\n- tests proving invalidation affects downstream report status.\n\n## Boundary\n\nFP-META-031 defines invalidation propagation semantics only.\n\nIt does not implement propagation, change schema, mutate historical reports, or mark existing artifacts stale.\nEOF\npython - <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np = Path('docs/forgepilot-progress-checklist.md')\ns = p.read_text()\nrow = '| Invalidation propagation rules | `COMPLETE` | `FP-META-031` | Downstream invalidation semantics are documented in `docs/invalidation-propagation-rules.md`; ForgePilot purpose and mission.\n2. Non-goals.\n3. System roles and ownership boundaries.\n4. Truth model: SQLite, Markdown, Git, server workspace, and GPT house.\n5. Evidence lifecycle.\n6. MCP role and tool layers.\n7. OpenCode harness role.\n8. GPT orchestration role.\n9. Executor, auditor, comparator, and human roles.\n10. Safety and approval model.\n11. Metrics, telemetry, validation, and admission model.\n12. Model routing future and constraints.\n13. Development phases from current state to evidence-based orchestration.\n14. Conditions required before routing or automation can influence decisions.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Do not replace existing standards documents.\n- Reference existing docs as supporting standards where appropriate.\n- Do not add implementation code.\n- Do not change schemas.\n- Do not start or stop OpenCode.\n- Do not claim that routing or autonomy exists today.\n- Preserve the principle that ForgePilot records observations, not narratives.\n- Preserve the principle that only admitted evidence may influence future decisions.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n1. `docs/forgepilot-master-plan.md` exists.\n2. The document clearly states ForgePilot's purpose.\n3. The document distinguishes project, tools, execution harness, and observatory.\n4. The document includes a phased roadmap.\n5. The document defines what must be true before evidence-based routing.\n6. The document names FP-MCP-148, FP-MCP-149, and FP-MCP-150 in the near-term roadmap.\n7. The document explains that direct GPT terminal access is an operator interface, not the product.\nEOF\ncat > docs/forgepilot-master-plan.md <<'EOF'\n# ForgePilot Master Plan\n\n## 1. Purpose\n\nForgePilot is a software production observatory for AI-assisted development.\n\nIts purpose is to turn AI-assisted software work into an observable, auditable, evidence-producing process.\n\nForgePilot exists to answer one long-term question:\n\n> What actually causes successful AI-assisted software delivery?\n\nIt answers that question by recording packets, executions, verification, audits, comparisons, telemetry, and admission decisions over time.\n\nForgePilot does not optimize for favorable stories. It records observations.\n\nForgePilot does not trust model claims by default. It requires evidence.\n\nForgePilot does not route work from vibes. Future routing may only use admitted evidence.\n\n## 2. Non-goals\n\nForgePilot is not:\n\n- an agent framework,\n- a benchmark leaderboard,\n- a generic model router,\n- a chat memory system,\n- a terminal remote-control tool,\n- a pile of automation scripts,\n- a replacement for verification, audit, or human judgment.\n\nForgePilot may use agents, models, terminals, MCP tools, OpenCode, and scripts, but those are tools. The product is the observatory.\n\n## 3. System roles\n\n### GPT\n\nGPT is the orchestrator, architect, reviewer, and run director.\n\nGPT may request bounded actions, inspect workspace state, draft packets, review diffs, compare evidence, and decide what to ask executors or auditors to do.\n\nGPT owns no durable truth.\n\n### ForgePilot repository\n\nThe ForgePilot repository is the evidence ledger and policy workspace.\n\nIt stores packets, run artifacts, docs, standards, metrics artifacts, and source code for the observatory itself.\n\n### SQLite\n\nSQLite is the intended runtime and environment truth layer.\n\nIt should eventually store normalized packet state, evidence records, metrics, admissions, telemetry, model outcomes, and routing signals.\n\n### Markdown\n\nMarkdown is policy and knowledge truth.\n\nArchitecture decisions, standards, plans, packets, audit results, comparison records, and rationale documents live in Markdown unless they need normalized runtime querying.\n\n### MCP bridge\n\nThe MCP bridge is the controlled capability boundary between GPT and the workspace.\n\nIt exposes tools for reading state, creating request artifacts, requesting approved terminal commands, validating preflight gates, and eventually invoking bounded worker runs.\n\nThe MCP bridge is not the observatory. It is an interface to the observatory and workspace.\n\n### OpenCode\n\nOpenCode is the execution harness.\n\nIt provides access to open-weight or external models that can act as executors, auditors, comparators, or specialists.\n\nOpenCode should perform bounded tasks and return artifacts. It should not own truth.\n\n### Open-weight models\n\nOpen-weight models are replaceable workers.\n\nThey may execute packets, audit implementations, compare outputs, classify failures, or summarize telemetry. Their claims become evidence only after validation and admission.\n\n### Human operator\n\nThe human operator owns authorization, policy judgment, and final responsibility.\n\nHuman approval may authorize bounded execution, but human approval alone does not convert output into admitted evidence.\n\n### Server workspace\n\nThe server workspace is the live lab bench.\n\nIt includes repositories, services, processes, listeners, local tool state, OpenCode state, and the GPT house.\n\nThe workspace is source-of-truth for live operational state, but only recorded and admitted artifacts become observatory evidence.\n\n### GPT house\n\nThe GPT house is a non-secret server-side orientation space for cross-session memory.\n\nIt helps future sessions recover context, but it is not admitted evidence by itself.\n\n## 4. Truth model\n\nForgePilot uses layered truth:\n\n1. Git records committed repository history.\n2. Markdown records policy, packets, rationale, audits, and plans.\n3. SQLite records normalized operational truth.\n4. The server workspace records live operational state.\n5. The GPT house records orientation memory.\n6. Model context is not durable truth.\n\nThe core rule remains:\n\n> The environment owns truth. Agents own no truth.\n\n## 5. Evidence lifecycle\n\nThe target lifecycle is:\n\n```text\nDRAFT\nREADY\nREQUESTED\nEXECUTED\nVERIFIED\nAUDITED\nCOMPARED\nADMISSION_REVIEWED\nADMITTED / REJECTED / QUARANTINED\n```\n\nA packet defines intent before execution.\n\nA request binds a packet to model, mode, workspace, and commit.\n\nExecution produces artifacts.\n\nVerification checks observable behavior.\n\nAudit evaluates correctness and constraint adherence.\n\nComparison evaluates multiple valid outputs when applicable.\n\nAdmission decides whether the evidence may influence observatory outputs.\n\nRouting decisions may only use admitted evidence.\n\n## 6. MCP role\n\nThe MCP bridge should evolve into four tool layers.\n\n### Operator tools\n\nOperator tools help GPT inspect and maintain the workspace:\n\n- read repository status,\n- read packets and runs,\n- inspect service state,\n- record handoffs,\n- create workspace snapshots,\n- run small approved terminal commands.\n\n### Execution tools\n\nExecution tools create and run bounded work:\n\n- create OpenCode request artifacts,\n- bind run requests to packet, commit, workspace, model, and mode,\n- start bounded worker runs only after gates pass,\n- capture executor outputs.\n\n### Evidence tools\n\nEvidence tools record what happened:\n\n- execution artifacts,\n- verification artifacts,\n- audit artifacts,\n- comparison artifacts,\n- metrics artifacts,\n- telemetry artifacts,\n- admission events.\n\n### Policy tools\n\nPolicy tools determine whether artifacts are usable:\n\n- validate packets,\n- validate requests,\n- validate provenance,\n- classify outcomes,\n- evaluate admission eligibility,\n- query admitted evidence,\n- support future routing recommendations.\n\n## 7. Direct terminal access\n\nDirect GPT terminal access is an operator interface, not the product.\n\nIt is useful for small, explicit, approved commands:\n\n- status checks,\n- verification commands,\n- commits and pushes,\n- service restarts,\n- small handoff artifacts.\n\nIt should not replace packet discipline, OpenCode execution, audit, telemetry, or admission.\n\nLarge edits, bulk telemetry capture, and multi-file changes should prefer OpenCode worker runs or explicitly bounded packets.\n\n## 8. OpenCode harness role\n\nOpenCode is the worker harness beneath GPT orchestration.\n\nThe desired flow is:\n\n```text\nGPT selects or drafts packet\nForgePilot records request\nOpenCode runs selected model as executor\nForgePilot records output\nAnother model or GPT audits\nForgePilot records audit\nComparison/admission determines evidence value\n```\n\nOpenCode should not be treated as the source of truth. Its local state should be inventoried and preserved where relevant, then normalized into ForgePilot evidence.\n\n## 9. Model roles\n\n### Executor\n\nAn executor implements a packet under constraints.\n\n### Auditor\n\nAn auditor reviews an executor result against packet requirements, acceptance criteria, and boundaries.\n\n### Comparator\n\nA comparator evaluates multiple accepted outputs and records relative strengths.\n\n### Classifier\n\nA classifier may label task type, failure mode, risk, or routing eligibility, but classification must follow observation.\n\n### Router\n\nA future router may recommend models or workflows only from admitted evidence.\n\n## 10. Safety model\n\nSafety is layered:\n\n1. Human approval for bounded actions.\n2. Workspace allowlists.\n3. Disable switches.\n4. Terminal-specific kill switch.\n5. Command policy and rate limits.\n6. Preflight validation.\n7. Evidence recording.\n8. Audit and admission gates.\n9. Network and service boundaries.\n10. Human override.\n\nApproval authorizes an action. It does not prove the action succeeded, and it does not admit evidence.\n\n## 11. Metrics, telemetry, validation, and admission\n\nMetrics and telemetry are observations.\n\nThey may be incomplete, untrusted, or structurally invalid.\n\nForgePilot must distinguish:\n\n- trust tier,\n- validation state,\n- admission state,\n- provenance completeness,\n- evidence usefulness,\n- routing eligibility.\n\nExisting standards support this model:\n\n- `docs/model-evaluation-harness.md`\n- `docs/metrics-trust-and-validation.md`\n- `docs/telemetry-authority-and-field-ownership.md`\n- `docs/persistence-standards.md`\n- `docs/model-outcome-recording-standards.md`\n- `docs/task-classification-standards.md`\n\n## 12. Routing future\n\nRouting is not the current goal. Evidence collection is the current goal.\n\nRouting becomes valid only when ForgePilot has enough admitted evidence to support recommendations.\n\nRouting must not use raw model claims, unvalidated telemetry, rejected artifacts, quarantined records, or narrative summaries without evidence.\n\nFuture routing may answer:\n\n- which model performs best for this task class,\n- which auditor catches this failure mode,\n- which workflow has the lowest correction rate,\n- which packet patterns create ambiguity,\n- when to escalate to a stronger model or human.\n\n## 13. Development phases\n\n### Phase 0 — Manual evidence\n\nPackets, manual execution, manual verification, manual audit, Markdown artifacts.\n\n### Phase 1 — SQLite observatory\n\nNormalize packets, runs, metrics, evidence records, validation states, and admission states.\n\n### Phase 2 — MCP-controlled workspace\n\nExpose controlled tools for GPT to inspect and operate the workspace with approval and evidence.\n\n### Phase 3 — OpenCode worker harness\n\nAllow GPT to create bounded OpenCode worker requests and collect structured results.\n\n### Phase 4 — Executor/auditor evaluations\n\nRun multiple models under controlled packets with cross-model audit and comparison.\n\n### Phase 5 — Evidence admission pipeline\n\nAdmit, reject, or quarantine evidence through explicit gates.\n\n### Phase 6 — Evidence-based recommendations\n\nUse admitted evidence to recommend models, workflows, packet improvements, and audit strategies.\n\n### Phase 7 — Adaptive orchestration\n\nAllow GPT to orchestrate more of the workflow, still bounded by packet discipline, safety gates, and admitted evidence.\n\n## 14. Required tool roadmap\n\nForgePilot and the MCP bridge need these tool families:\n\n1. Workspace state snapshot tools.\n2. Terminal command tools with throttling and policy.\n3. OpenCode request and worker invocation tools.\n4. Telemetry capture tools.\n5. Evidence artifact recording tools.\n6. Verification recording tools.\n7. Audit recording tools.\n8. Comparison recording tools.\n9. Admission review tools.\n10. SQLite query tools for admitted evidence.\n11. Model outcome and task classification tools.\n12. Handoff and GPT house orientation tools.\n\n## 15. Near-term roadmap\n\n### FP-MCP-148 — Terminal MCP Practical Hardening\n\nImplement terminal kill switch, Node/pnpm PATH fix, and container-engine command blocks.\n\n### FP-MCP-149 — OpenCode Local Telemetry Inventory\n\nInventory local OpenCode telemetry metadata before stopping or restarting the long-running OpenCode server.\n\n### FP-MCP-150 — ForgePilot Master Plan\n\nRecord this comprehensive plan before further expansion.\n\n### FP-MCP-151 — Terminal Rate Limiting and Command Shaping\n\nReduce rapid-fire command patterns and force large tasks toward worker runs.\n\n### FP-MCP-152 — Workspace State Snapshot\n\nCapture live server workspace state as operational truth: repos, services, processes, listeners, OpenCode state, and GPT house state.\n\n### FP-MCP-153 — OpenCode Worker Invocation v1\n\nAllow GPT to create and monitor bounded OpenCode worker tasks through MCP.\n\n### FP-MCP-154 — OpenCode Telemetry Capture v1\n\nCapture token, cost, duration, model, and tool-use telemetry into ForgePilot evidence where available.\n\n### FP-MCP-155 — Executor/Auditor Harness v1\n\nRun one model as executor and another as auditor under a controlled ForgePilot packet.\n\n### FP-MCP-156 — Evidence Admission Query v1\n\nExpose admitted evidence queries for future routing and orchestration decisions.\n\n## 16. Conditions before routing or automation\n\nBefore routing or automation can influence decisions, ForgePilot must have:\n\n1. Structured evidence records.\n2. Provenance completeness checks.\n3. Validation states.\n4. Admission states.\n5. Reliable model identity recording.\n6. Task classification standards applied consistently.\n7. Outcome recording standards applied consistently.\n8. Audit artifacts for relevant runs.\n9. Comparison artifacts for multi-model runs.\n10. SQLite-backed queries over admitted evidence.\n11. Clear exclusion of rejected and quarantined evidence.\n12. Human override and review paths.\n\nUntil those conditions are met, GPT may recommend next steps, but ForgePilot must not present routing as evidence-based automation.\n\n## 17. North star\n\nThe final goal is not for GPT to do everything directly.\n\nThe final goal is evidence-based orchestration:\n\n```text\nGPT orchestrates.\nOpenCode executes.\nModels perform bounded work.\nForgePilot observes.\nAudits evaluate.\nSQLite remembers.\nAdmission controls what future decisions may use.\n```\n\nForgePilot succeeds when future decisions are not based on memory, preference, or vibes, but on admitted evidence from real software production workflows.\nEOF\ngit add packets/FP-MCP-150.md docs/forgepilot-master-plan.md\ngit commit -m \"Add ForgePilot master plan\"\ngit push",

Evidence lifecycle\n\nThe target lifecycle is:\n\n```text\nDRAFT\nREADY\nREQUESTED\nEXECUTED\nVERIFIED\nAUDITED\nCOMPARED\nADMISSION_REVIEWED\nADMITTED / REJECTED / QUARANTINED\n```\n\nA packet defines intent before execution.\n\nA request binds a packet to model, mode, workspace, and commit.\n\nExecution produces artifacts.\n\nVerification checks observable behavior.\n\nAudit evaluates correctness and constraint adherence.\n\nComparison evaluates multiple valid outputs when applicable.\n\nAdmission decides whether the evidence may influence observatory outputs.\n\nRouting decisions may only use admitted evidence.\n\n## 6. MCP role\n\nThe MCP bridge should evolve into four tool layers.\n\n### Operator tools\n\nOperator tools help GPT inspect and maintain the workspace:\n\n- read repository status,\n- read packets and runs,\n- inspect service state,\n- record handoffs,\n- create workspace snapshots,\n- run small approved terminal commands.\n\n### Execution tools\n\nExecution tools create and run bounded work:\n\n- create OpenCode request artifacts,\n- bind run requests to packet, commit, workspace, model, and mode,\n- start bounded worker runs only after gates pass,\n- capture executor outputs.\n\n### Evidence tools\n\nEvidence tools record what happened:\n\n- execution artifacts,\n- verification artifacts,\n- audit artifacts,\n- comparison artifacts,\n- metrics artifacts,\n- telemetry artifacts,\n- admission events.\n\n### Policy tools\n\nPolicy tools determine whether artifacts are usable:\n\n- validate packets,\n- validate requests,\n- validate provenance,\n- classify outcomes,\n- evaluate admission eligibility,\n- query admitted evidence,\n- support future routing recommendations.\n\n## 7. Direct terminal access\n\nDirect GPT terminal access is an operator interface, not the product.\n\nIt is useful for small, explicit, approved commands:\n\n- status checks,\n- verification commands,\n- commits and pushes,\n- service restarts,\n- small handoff artifacts.\n\nIt should not replace packet discipline, OpenCode execution, audit, telemetry, or admission.\n\nLarge edits, bulk telemetry capture, and multi-file changes should prefer OpenCode worker runs or explicitly bounded packets.\n\n## 8. OpenCode harness role\n\nOpenCode is the worker harness beneath GPT orchestration.\n\nThe desired flow is:\n\n```text\nGPT selects or drafts packet\nForgePilot records request\nOpenCode runs selected model as executor\nForgePilot records output\nAnother model or GPT audits\nForgePilot records audit\nComparison/admission determines evidence value\n```\n\nOpenCode should not be treated as the source of truth. Its local state should be inventoried and preserved where relevant, then normalized into ForgePilot evidence.\n\n## 9. Model roles\n\n### Executor\n\nAn executor implements a packet under constraints.\n\n### Auditor\n\nAn auditor reviews an executor result against packet requirements, acceptance criteria, and boundaries.\n\n### Comparator\n\nA comparator evaluates multiple accepted outputs and records relative strengths.\n\n### Classifier\n\nA classifier may label task type, failure mode, risk, or routing eligibility, but classification must follow observation.\n\n### Router\n\nA future router may recommend models or workflows only from admitted evidence.\n\n## 10. Safety model\n\nSafety is layered:\n\n1. Human approval for bounded actions.\n2. Workspace allowlists.\n3. Disable switches.\n4. Terminal-specific kill switch.\n5. Command policy and rate limits.\n6. Preflight validation.\n7. Evidence recording.\n8. Audit and admission gates.\n9. Network and service boundaries.\n10. Human override.\n\nApproval authorizes an action. It does not prove the action succeeded, and it does not admit evidence.\n\n## 11. Metrics, telemetry, validation, and admission\n\nMetrics and telemetry are observations.\n\nThey may be incomplete, untrusted, or structurally invalid.\n\nForgePilot must distinguish:\n\n- trust tier,\n- validation state,\n- admission state,\n- provenance completeness,\n- evidence usefulness,\n- routing eligibility.\n\nExisting standards support this model:\n\n- `docs/model-evaluation-harness.md`\n- `docs/metrics-trust-and-validation.md`\n- `docs/telemetry-authority-and-field-ownership.md`\n- `docs/persistence-standards.md`\n- `docs/model-outcome-recording-standards.md`\n- `docs/task-classification-standards.md`\n\n## 12. Routing future\n\nRouting is not the current goal. Evidence collection is the current goal.\n\nRouting becomes valid only when ForgePilot has enough admitted evidence to support recommendations.\n\nRouting must not use raw model claims, unvalidated telemetry, rejected artifacts, quarantined records, or narrative summaries without evidence.\n\nFuture routing may answer:\n\n- which model performs best for this task class,\n- which auditor catches this failure mode,\n- which workflow has the lowest correction rate,\n- which packet patterns create ambiguity,\n- when to escalate to a stronger model or human.\n\n## 13. Development phases\n\n### Phase 0 — Manual evidence\n\nPackets, manual execution, manual verification, manual audit, Markdown artifacts.\n\n### Phase 1 — SQLite observatory\n\nNormalize packets, runs, metrics, evidence records, validation states, and admission states.\n\n### Phase 2 — MCP-controlled workspace\n\nExpose controlled tools for GPT to inspect and operate the workspace with approval and evidence.\n\n### Phase 3 — OpenCode worker harness\n\nAllow GPT to create bounded OpenCode worker requests and collect structured results.\n\n### Phase 4 — Executor/auditor evaluations\n\nRun multiple models under controlled packets with cross-model audit and comparison.\n\n### Phase 5 — Evidence admission pipeline\n\nAdmit, reject, or quarantine evidence through explicit gates.\n\n### Phase 6 — Evidence-based recommendations\n\nUse admitted evidence to recommend models, workflows, packet improvements, and audit strategies.\n\n### Phase 7 — Adaptive orchestration\n\nAllow GPT to orchestrate more of the workflow, still bounded by packet discipline, safety gates, and admitted evidence.\n\n## 14. Required tool roadmap\n\nForgePilot and the MCP bridge need these tool families:\n\n1. Workspace state snapshot tools.\n2. Terminal command tools with throttling and policy.\n3. OpenCode request and worker invocation tools.\n4. Telemetry capture tools.\n5. Evidence artifact recording tools.\n6. Verification recording tools.\n7. Audit recording tools.\n8. Comparison recording tools.\n9. Admission review tools.\n10. SQLite query tools for admitted evidence.\n11. Model outcome and task classification tools.\n12. Handoff and GPT house orientation tools.\n\n## 15. Near-term roadmap\n\n### FP-MCP-148 — Terminal MCP Practical Hardening\n\nImplement terminal kill switch, Node/pnpm PATH fix, and container-engine command blocks.\n\n### FP-MCP-149 — OpenCode Local Telemetry Inventory\n\nInventory local OpenCode telemetry metadata before stopping or restarting the long-running OpenCode server.\n\n### FP-MCP-150 — ForgePilot Master Plan\n\nRecord this comprehensive plan before further expansion.\n\n### FP-MCP-151 — Terminal Rate Limiting and Command Shaping\n\nReduce rapid-fire command patterns and force large tasks toward worker runs.\n\n### FP-MCP-152 — Workspace State Snapshot\n\nCapture live server workspace state as operational truth: repos, services, processes, listeners, OpenCode state, and GPT house state.\n\n### FP-MCP-153 — OpenCode Worker Invocation v1\n\nAllow GPT to create and monitor bounded OpenCode worker tasks through MCP.\n\n### FP-MCP-154 — OpenCode Telemetry Capture v1\n\nCapture token, cost, duration, model, and tool-use telemetry into ForgePilot evidence where available.\n\n### FP-MCP-155 — Executor/Auditor Harness v1\n\nRun one model as executor and another as auditor under a controlled ForgePilot packet.\n\n### FP-MCP-156 — Evidence Admission Query v1\n\nExpose admitted evidence queries for future routing and orchestration decisions.\n\n## 16. Conditions before routing or automation\n\nBefore routing or automation can influence decisions, ForgePilot must have:\n\n1. Structured evidence records.\n2. Provenance completeness checks.\n3. Validation states.\n4. Admission states.\n5. Reliable model identity recording.\n6. Task classification standards applied consistently.\n7. Outcome recording standards applied consistently.\n8. Audit artifacts for relevant runs.\n9. Comparison artifacts for multi-model runs.\n10. SQLite-backed queries over admitted evidence.\n11. Clear exclusion of rejected and quarantined evidence.\n12. Human override and review paths.\n\nUntil those conditions are met, GPT may recommend next steps, but ForgePilot must not present routing as evidence-based automation.\n\n## 17. North star\n\nThe final goal is not for GPT to do everything directly.\n\nThe final goal is evidence-based orchestration:\n\n```text\nGPT orchestrates.\nOpenCode executes.\nModels perform bounded work.\nForgePilot observes.\nAudits evaluate.\nSQLite remembers.\nAdmission controls what future decisions may use.\n```\n\nForgePilot succeeds when future decisions are not based on memory, preference, or vibes, but on admitted evidence from real software production workflows.\nEOF\ngit add packets/FP-MCP-150.md docs/forgepilot-master-plan.md\ngit commit -m \"Add ForgePilot master plan\"\ngit push",

Meaning comes later through admission, reporting, and comparison packets.\n\n## Scope\n\nImplement one read-only MCP tool in:\n\n```text\n/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp\n```\n\nand any required ForgePilot-side CLI/database query support in:\n\n```text\n/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot\n```\n\n## Required MCP Tool\n\nAdd:\n\n```text\nforgepilot_query_persisted_opencode_worker_telemetry\n```\n\n## Required Inputs\n\nAll inputs are optional unless otherwise stated:\n\n- `packetId?: string`\n- `workerStatus?: string`\n- `terminalState?: string`\n- `validationState?: string`\n- `admissionState?: string`\n- `limit?: number`\n\n## Query Semantics\n\nThe tool must query only the persisted SQLite table:\n\n```text\nopencode_worker_telemetry\n```\n\nIt must not read live worker artifacts, recapture telemetry, or inspect raw OpenCode state.\n\n## Allowed Filters\n\nThe tool may filter by:\n\n- packet id,\n- worker status,\n- terminal state,\n- validation state,\n- admission state.\n\nThe tool may support a bounded limit.\n\nRecommended default limit:\n\n```text\n20\n```\n\nRecommended maximum limit:\n\n```text\n100\n```\n\n## Required Output Shape\n\nReturn structured JSON similar to:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"rows\": [\n    {\n      \"packet_id\": \"FP-MCP-153\",\n      \"worker_id\": \"WORKER-...\",\n      \"worker_status\": \"SUCCEEDED\",\n      \"terminal_state\": \"TERMINAL_SUCCEEDED\",\n      \"trust_tier\": \"TIER_2_VERIFIED_ARTIFACT\",\n      \"validation_state\": \"VALID\",\n      \"admission_state\": \"NOT_EVALUATED\",\n      \"persisted_at\": \"...\"\n    }\n  ],\n  \"count\": 1,\n  \"filters\": {\n    \"packetId\": \"FP-MCP-153\",\n    \"validationState\": \"VALID\"\n  },\n  \"limit\": 20,\n  \"observation_semantics\": \"Persisted worker telemetry query results are observations, not admitted evidence or routing input.\"\n}\n```\n\n## Required Row Fields\n\nEach returned row should include at minimum:\n\n- `packet_id`\n- `worker_id`\n- `schema_version`\n- `captured_at`\n- `persisted_at`\n- `worker_status`\n- `terminal_state`\n- `provenance_completeness`\n- `trust_tier`\n- `validation_state`\n- `admission_state`\n- `duration_ms`\n- `target_workspace_id`\n- `model_id`\n- `telemetry_path`\n\nThe tool may omit long fields such as full command args unless explicitly included later by another packet.\n\n## Safety Requirements\n\nThe tool must not:\n\n- mutate SQLite rows,\n- persist new telemetry,\n- recapture telemetry,\n- start workers,\n- stop workers,\n- restart workers,\n- attach to the legacy OpenCode server,\n- read raw OpenCode session databases,\n- infer model quality,\n- rank models,\n- make routing decisions,\n- change admission state,\n- treat query results as admitted evidence.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\nThis packet does not implement:\n\n- evidence admission,\n- routing,\n- model comparison,\n- dashboards,\n- reporting summaries,\n- telemetry aggregation,\n- score calculation,\n- cost/token analysis,\n- batch backfill,\n- worker lifecycle control.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n1. Build passes.\n2. Query tool is visible after connector refresh.\n3. Query without filters returns persisted telemetry rows up to the default limit.\n4. Query by packet id works.\n5. Query by worker status works.\n6. Query by terminal state works.\n7. Query by validation state works.\n8. Query by admission state works.\n9. Limit is bounded and enforced.\n10. Query result includes `observation_semantics`.\n11. Query result does not mutate the database.\n12. Query result does not start, stop, or inspect live workers.\n13. Existing persisted FP-MCP-153 worker telemetry can be returned.\n14. ForgePilot and bridge repositories are clean after evidence is recorded.\n\n## Verification Requirements\n\nVerify at minimum:\n\n- ForgePilot typecheck,\n- bridge build,\n- query with no filters,\n- query with `packetId = FP-MCP-153`,\n- query with `workerStatus = SUCCEEDED`,\n- query with `terminalState = TERMINAL_SUCCEEDED`,\n- query with `validationState = VALID`,\n- query with `admissionState = NOT_EVALUATED`,\n- bounded limit behavior,\n- direct MCP query after connector refresh,\n- final clean-state check.\n\n## Expected Result\n\nGPT can query persisted worker telemetry observations safely through MCP.\n\nTelemetry becomes visible and inspectable, but not meaningful in the admission, routing, or model-quality sense.\n\nNo aggregation is introduced in this packet. A simple filtered row query is the intended next step.\nEOF\n\ngit status --short packets/FP-MCP-157.md\ngit diff -- packets/FP-MCP-157.md",

runs/FP-META-030/terminal-commands/TERM-20260702T105034128Z-275b320a-request.json:7:  "command": "cat > docs/evidence-root-and-workspace-boundary.md <<'EOF'\n# Evidence Root and Workspace Boundary\n\n## Purpose\n\nDefine where ForgePilot core evidence ends, where project workspace evidence begins, and how reconstruction/reporting must scope its inputs.\n\n## Core Rule\n\n```text\nA project workspace artifact is not ForgePilot core evidence unless a report explicitly scopes that project workspace as an input.\n```\n\n## Terms\n\n| Term | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| ForgePilot core repository | The source repository containing ForgePilot implementation, core docs, migrations, tests, root packets, and core governance evidence. |\n| ForgePilot evidence root | The explicit filesystem root from which a report/reconstruction command reads evidence. |\n| Project workspace root | A root containing project-specific packets, plans, runs, audits, comparisons, observations, and model outputs. |\n| Instrument-project workspace | A project workspace for tooling used to operate ForgePilot, such as the ChatGPT MCP bridge. |\n| Observed-project workspace | A project workspace for external software being observed by ForgePilot. |\n| Bootstrap internal workspace | Historical project workspace content currently stored under the ForgePilot core repository during bootstrap. |\n\n## Current Bootstrap Internal Workspace\n\nThe current bootstrap internal workspace is:\n\n```text\ninstrument-projects/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp/\n```\n\nThis content is historical bootstrap evidence.\n\nIt must be preserved, not casually renamed, deleted, or moved.\n\n## Future Preferred Layout\n\nThe preferred future layout is:\n\n```text\n/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot\n  # ForgePilot core repository\n\n/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot-workspaces\n  # ForgePilot-managed project/evidence workspaces\n\n/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp\n  # Actual MCP bridge source repository\n```\n\nProject-specific packets, plans, checklists, runs, audits, comparisons, observations, and model outputs should eventually live under a project workspace root outside the ForgePilot core repository.\n\n## Reconstruction and Reporting Scope\n\nCore ForgePilot reports may read core evidence from explicit root-relative locations such as:\n\n```text\npackets/\nruns/\ndocs/\n```\n\nProject workspace reports may read project evidence only when the project workspace root is explicitly selected.\n\nCore reconstruction must not silently include:\n\n```text\ninstrument-projects/\nobserved-projects/\n```\n\nunless a report explicitly declares that project workspace as input.\n\n## Bootstrap Evidence Rule\n\nBootstrap internal workspace evidence may remain in the ForgePilot core repository for historical continuity.\n\nHowever, its existence must not cause core V1 reports to treat project-specific packets or runs as core packets or runs.\n\n## Current Conformance Status\n\nCurrent conformance is evaluated in:\n\n```text\nruns/FP-META-030/reconstruction-path-boundary-check.txt\nruns/FP-META-030/path-search-results.txt\nruns/FP-META-030/reporting-source-excerpts.txt\n```\n\nThe boundary rule is defined here, but operational conformance must be checked against code and not inferred from this document alone.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\nThis document does not migrate files, implement external workspace support, project workspace artifact is not ForgePilot core evidence unless a report explicitly scopes that project workspace as an input.\n```\n\n## Terms\n\n| Term | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| ForgePilot core filesystem root from which a report/reconstruction command reads evidence. |\n| Project workspace root | A root containing project-specific packets, plans, runs, audits, comparisons, observations, and model outputs. |\n| Instrument-project workspace | A project workspace for tooling used to operate ForgePilot, such as the ChatGPT MCP bridge. |\n| Observed-project workspace | A project workspace for external software being observed by ForgePilot. |\n| Bootstrap internal workspace | Historical project workspace content currently stored under the ForgePilot core repository during bootstrap. |\n\n## repository\n```\n\nProject-specific packets, plans, checklists, runs, audits, comparisons, observations, and model outputs should eventually live under a project workspace root outside the ForgePilot runs/FP-META-031/terminal-commands/TERM-20260702T105854074Z-815fa4e0-request.json:7:  "command": "cat > docs/invalidation-propagation-rules.md <<'EOF'\n# Invalidation Propagation Rules\n\n## Purpose\n\nDefine how ForgePilot treats downstream artifacts when admitted evidence is later invalidated, quarantined, or otherwise defeated.\n\n## Core Rule\n\n```text\nInvalidation must not only revoke evidence authority.\nIt must also identify downstream artifacts whose claims may now be stale.\n```\n\nA revocation mechanism that only logs invalidation but leaves dependent reports unmarked is incomplete.\n\n## Relationship to Evidence Root Boundary\n\nFP-META-030 defines evidence-root and workspace boundaries.\n\nInvalidation propagation follows those boundaries:\n\n- core evidence invalidation affects core downstream artifacts that explicitly depended on that evidence,\n- project workspace invalidation affects project workspace downstream artifacts that explicitly depended on that evidence,\n- cross-workspace propagation occurs only through explicit dependency edges,\n- artifacts are not rewritten silently across boundaries.\n\n## Terms\n\n| Term | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| Invalidation source | The evidence record, admission event, validation result, review request, or invalidation event that revokes or defeats prior authority. |\n| Dependency edge | An explicit recorded relationship showing that one artifact relied on another artifact or evidence record. |\n| Downstream artifact | Any report, summary, comparison, admission decision, checklist update, model trust assignment, or derived record that depends on earlier evidence. |\n| Stale report | A report whose input evidence changed or was invalidated after the report was produced. |\n| Affected artifact | A downstream artifact that may have depended on invalidated evidence. |\n| Requires reconstruction | A derived artifact whose current claims must be regenerated or re-evaluated before use. |\n\n## Required Downstream States\n\nFuture propagation-capable reports or ledgers should be able to express:\n\n| State | Meaning |\n|---|---|\n| `NOT_AFFECTED` | No explicit dependency on invalidated evidence is known. |\n| `AFFECTED_BY_INVALIDATION` | Artifact has an explicit dependency on invalidated/quarantined evidence. |\n| `STALE_REPORT` | Report was generated before an invalidation that affects one or more inputs. |\n| `REQUIRES_RECONSTRUCTION` | Artifact must be regenerated or re-evaluated before it can be used as current evidence. |\n| `SUPERSEDED_BY_RECONSTRUCTION` | Artifact has been replaced by a later reconstruction that accounts for invalidation. |\n| `UNKNOWN_DEPENDENCY_STATUS` | Dependency edges are insufficient to determine impact. |\n\n## Propagation Semantics\n\nWhen evidence is invalidated or quarantined:\n\n1. The invalidation event defeats the authority of the targeted admission where current admission logic says it does.\n2. ForgePilot must identify artifacts with explicit dependency edges to the invalidated evidence.\n3. Dependent reports should be marked `STALE_REPORT` or `AFFECTED_BY_INVALIDATION`.\n4. Derived artifacts that may be reused for future decisions should be marked `REQUIRES_RECONSTRUCTION`.\n5. New reports must account for the invalidation before claiming current status.\n6. Historical reports must not be edited in place; later artifacts must record that they were affected or superseded.\n\n## Cross-Workspace Propagation\n\nInvalidation does not cross workspace boundaries by default.\n\nPropagation from core to project workspace, project workspace to core, or project workspace to project workspace requires explicit dependency edges.\n\nExamples:\n\n| Scenario | Propagation |\n|---|---|\n| Core report depends only on core packets/runs | Core invalidation may affect it. |\n| Instrument-project report depends only on instrument workspace evidence | Instrument invalidation may affect it. |\n| Core V1 report explicitly includes instrument workspace evidence | Relevant instrument invalidation may affect the core report. |\n| Instrument workspace file merely exists under bootstrap internal path | No propagation unless explicitly referenced. |\n\n## Non-Propagation Cases\n\nInvalidation must not automatically affect:\n\n- artifacts with no explicit dependency edge,\n- reports produced from a different evidence root,\n- project workspaces not selected as inputs,\n- historical artifacts that merely coexist in the repository,\n- checklist rows that do not cite the invalidated evidence.\n\nIf dependency status is unclear, the correct state is:\n\n```text\nUNKNOWN_DEPENDENCY_STATUS\n```\n\nnot `NOT_AFFECTED`.\n\n## Append-Only Requirement\n\nPropagation must be append-only.\n\nForgePilot must not rewrite old reports to erase prior claims.\n\nInstead, it should append or generate later artifacts that say:\n\n```text\nThis artifact was affected by invalidation event X.\nThis artifact requires reconstruction.\nThis artifact was superseded by reconstruction Y.\n```\n\n## Model Trust Assignments\n\nModel trust assignments are downstream artifacts when they rely on prior model performance evidence.\n\nA model promotion or demotion must be append-only and provenance-bearing.\n\nIf a trust assignment is later shown to be wrong, the correction should record:\n\n- prior assignment,\n- invalidating evidence,\n- assigned_by or corrected_by actor,\n- assignment method,\n- affected task class,\n- affected project/context,\n- new trust state,\n- evidence references.\n\nA trust label must not be silently overwritten.\n\n## Current Implementation Status\n\n```text\nPROPAGATION_RULE_DEFINED_IMPLEMENTATION_NOT_PRESENT\n```\n\nCurrent code records admission invalidation events and derives eligibility using defeating invalidation events.\n\nCurrent source search did not find implemented downstream states such as:\n\n```text\nAFFECTED_BY_INVALIDATION\nSTALE_REPORT\nREQUIRES_RECONSTRUCTION\nSUPERSEDED_BY_RECONSTRUCTION\nUNKNOWN_DEPENDENCY_STATUS\n```\n\nTherefore FP-META-031 defines the required semantics but does not claim current implementation conformance.\n\n## Evidence Quality\n\nThe current implementation status is based on command-captured source/docs search in:\n\n```text\nruns/FP-META-031/invalidation-source-search.txt\n```\n\nTrust classification:\n\n```text\nTIER_1_STATIC_SOURCE_INSPECTION\n```\n\nThis is stronger than narrative self-report because search output and source excerpts are preserved. It is weaker than runtime tracing or tests proving propagation behavior.\n\n## Future Implementation Requirements\n\nA future implementation packet should define or implement:\n\n- dependency edge capture,\n- stale report markers,\n- affected artifact markers,\n- reconstruction requirement markers,\n- supersession markers,\n- report generation that checks invalidation events,\n- tests proving invalidation affects downstream report status.\n\n## Boundary\n\nFP-META-031 defines invalidation propagation semantics only.\n\nIt does not implement propagation, change schema, mutate historical reports, or mark existing artifacts stale.\nEOF\npython - <<'PY'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np = Path('docs/forgepilot-progress-checklist.md')\ns = p.read_text()\nrow = '| Invalidation propagation rules | `COMPLETE` | `FP-META-031` | Downstream invalidation semantics are documented in `docs/invalidation-propagation-rules.md`; current implementation status is `PROPAGATION_RULE_DEFINED_IMPLEMENTATION_NOT_PRESENT`. | Governance only; no propagation implementation yet. |'\nmarker = '| Evidence root and workspace boundary | `COMPLETE` | `FP-META-030` | Boundary rules are documented in `docs/evidence-root-and-workspace-boundary.md`; grep/source-excerpt path check recorded in `runs/FP-META-030/`. | Current code appears conformant, but runtime path tracing is not yet implemented. |'\nif row not in s and marker in s:\n    s = s.replace(marker, marker + '\\n' + row)\np.write_text(s)\nPY\ncat > runs/FP-META-031/verification.txt <<'EOF'\nFP-META-031 verification\n\nInvalidation propagation rules document exists:\nPASS\n- docs/invalidation-propagation-rules.md\n\nSource search artifact exists:\nPASS\n- runs/FP-META-031/invalidation-source-search.txt\n\nRequired concepts defined:\nPASS\n- invalidation source\n- dependency edge\n- downstream artifact\n- stale report\n- affected artifact\n- requires reconstruction\n- cross-workspace propagation\n- non-propagation cases\n- append-only propagation\n- model trust assignment propagation\n\nCurrent implementation status recorded:\nPASS\n- PROPAGATION_RULE_DEFINED_IMPLEMENTATION_NOT_PRESENT\n\nTrust tier recorded:\nPASS\n- TIER_1_STATIC_SOURCE_INSPECTION\n\nScope:\nPASS\n- no database schema changed\n- no propagation code implemented\n- no historical reports mutated\n- no files migrated\n- no implementation code changed\nEOF\nprintf '%s\\n' '--- status ---'\ngit status --short\nprintf '%s\\n' '--- rule headings ---'\ngrep -n \"^## \" docs/invalidation-propagation-rules.md\nprintf '%s\\n' '--- implementation status ---'\ngrep -n \"PROPAGATION_RULE\\|TIER_1_STATIC\\|AFFECTED_BY_INVALIDATION\\|STALE_REPORT\\|REQUIRES_RECONSTRUCTION\" docs/invalidation-propagation-rules.md runs/FP-META-031/verification.txt",
