and implement first-class telemetry capture for OpenCode worker runs started through the FP-MCP-153 worker invocation tools.\n\n## Goal\n\nTurn OpenCode worker run artifacts into normalized ForgePilot telemetry observations without treating them as admitted evidence or routing input.\n\nFP-MCP-154 should answer:\n\nWhat can ForgePilot safely observe about an OpenCode worker run after it starts and after it completes?\n\n## Background\n\nFP-MCP-153 introduced first-class worker lifecycle tools:\n\n- `forgepilot_start_opencode_worker`\n- `forgepilot_get_opencode_worker_status`\n- `forgepilot_read_opencode_worker_result`\n\nThose tools create worker directories under:\n\n`runs/<packetId>/opencode-workers/<workerId>/`\n\nEach worker directory contains start request, pid, stdout, stderr, and status artifacts.\n\nFP-MCP-154 adds a telemetry layer on top of those worker artifacts.\n\n## Scope\n\nImplement OpenCode worker telemetry capture v1 in:\n\n`/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp`\n\nThe implementation may add a dedicated module and small MCP registrations.\n\n## Required Tools\n\nAdd GPT-facing MCP tools:\n\n1. `forgepilot_capture_opencode_worker_telemetry`\n2. `forgepilot_read_opencode_worker_telemetry`\n\n## Tool 1: `forgepilot_capture_opencode_worker_telemetry`\n\n### Required Inputs\n\n- `packetId: string`\n- `workerId: string`\n- `approval: \"CAPTURE_OPENCODE_WORKER_TELEMETRY\"`\n\n### Requirements\n\nThe tool must:\n\n- require exact approval `CAPTURE_OPENCODE_WORKER_TELEMETRY`,\n- read an existing worker directory only,\n- refuse if the worker status artifact does not exist,\n- refresh/read worker status using existing worker status logic if possible,\n- read worker stdout/stderr metadata,\n- compute safe hashes for worker artifacts,\n- write normalized telemetry artifact under the worker directory,\n- return structured telemetry.\n\n### Required Artifact\n\nWrite:\n\n`runs/<packetId>/opencode-workers/<workerId>/telemetry.json`\n\nThe telemetry artifact must include at minimum:\n\n- schema version,\n- packet id,\n- worker id,\n- captured timestamp,\n- worker status,\n- started/completed timestamps when available,\n- duration milliseconds when computable,\n- target workspace id,\n- workspace path,\n- prompt file path,\n- prompt file sha256,\n- model id if present,\n- command executable path,\n- command args metadata,\n- stdout path,\n- stderr path,\n- stdout byte count,\n- stderr byte count,\n- stdout sha256,\n- stderr sha256,\n- status artifact sha256,\n- start request artifact sha256,\n- terminal state classification,\n- provenance completeness classification,\n- trust tier classification,\n- validation state classification,\n- admission state classification,\n- observation semantics.\n\n## Tool 2: `forgepilot_read_opencode_worker_telemetry`\n\n### Required Inputs\n\n- `packetId: string`\n- `workerId: string`\n\n### Requirements\n\nThe tool must:\n\n- read an existing telemetry artifact,\n- return structured telemetry,\n- not recapture or mutate telemetry,\n- refuse if telemetry is missing.\n\n## Classification Requirements\n\nTelemetry is an observation, not admitted evidence.\n\nInitial v1 classifications:\n\n- trust tier: `TIER_2_VERIFIED_ARTIFACT`\n- validation state: `VALID` when required artifacts exist and hashes can be computed, otherwise `INCOMPLETE`\n- admission state: `NOT_EVALUATED`\n\nTerminal state classification should be derived from worker status:\n\n- `SUCCEEDED` -> `TERMINAL_SUCCEEDED`\n- `FAILED` -> `TERMINAL_FAILED`\n- `UNKNOWN_EXIT` -> `TERMINAL_UNKNOWN_EXIT`\n- `RUNNING` -> `NON_TERMINAL_RUNNING`\n\nProvenance completeness should be:\n\n- `COMPLETE` when status, start request, stdout, stderr, prompt hash, worker id, packet id, workspace, and timestamps are present,\n- otherwise `INCOMPLETE`.\n\n## Safety Requirements\n\nThe implementation must not:\n\n- start workers,\n- stop workers,\n- restart services,\n- attach to the legacy OpenCode server,\n- read raw OpenCode session databases,\n- read secrets,\n- expose environment variables,\n- infer model performance quality,\n- admit evidence for routing.\n\nIt may read only worker artifacts created by FP-MCP-153.\n\n## Non-Goals\n\nThis packet does not implement:\n\n- cost/token parsing from OpenCode internal state,\n- SQLite persistence,\n- routing decisions,\n- model ranking,\n- admission review,\n- dashboarding,\n- broad MCP refactor,\n- telemetry ingestion from legacy OpenCode server state.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n1. MCP bridge build passes.\n2. Tool `forgepilot_capture_opencode_worker_telemetry` is visible after connector refresh.\n3. Tool `forgepilot_read_opencode_worker_telemetry` is visible after connector refresh.\n4. Invalid capture approval is rejected.\n5. Missing worker telemetry capture is rejected with a clear error.\n6. Capturing telemetry for an existing FP-MCP-153 worker creates `telemetry.json`.\n7. Telemetry includes status, duration, artifact paths, byte counts, hashes, classifications, and observation semantics.\n8. Read telemetry returns the captured artifact without mutation.\n9. No worker or service is started, stopped, or restarted by telemetry capture.\n10. ForgePilot and bridge repositories are clean after implementation evidence is recorded.\n\n## Verification Requirements\n\nVerify at minimum:\n\n- `pnpm build` in bridge repo,\n- invalid approval rejection,\n- missing worker rejection,\n- capture telemetry for a known FP-MCP-153 worker,\n- read telemetry for that worker,\n- verify telemetry JSON exists and is valid,\n- verify legacy OpenCode server remains running,\n- record implementation evidence under `runs/FP-MCP-154/`.\n\n## Expected Result\n\nForgePilot can capture normalized, safe, non-admitted telemetry observations from OpenCode worker runs.\n\nFP-MCP-154 prepares the system for future SQLite persistence and evidence admission work.\nEOF\n\ngit add packets/FP-MCP-154.md runs/FP-MCP-154/workspace-snapshots/SNAPSHOT-20260701T203118980Z-764da857.json\ngit commit -m \"Add FP-MCP-154 OpenCode telemetry packet\"\ngit push\ngit status --short",

ForgePilot purpose and mission.\n2. Non-goals.\n3. System roles and ownership boundaries.\n4. Truth model: SQLite, Markdown, Git, server workspace, and GPT house.\n5. Evidence lifecycle.\n6. MCP role and tool layers.\n7. OpenCode harness role.\n8. GPT orchestration role.\n9. Executor, auditor, comparator, and human roles.\n10. Safety and approval model.\n11. Metrics, telemetry, validation, and admission model.\n12. Model routing future and constraints.\n13. Development phases from current state to evidence-based orchestration.\n14. Required tool roadmap.\n15. Near-term packet roadmap.\n16. Conditions required before routing or automation can influence decisions.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Do not replace existing standards documents.\n- Reference existing docs as supporting standards where appropriate.\n- Do not add implementation code.\n- Do not change schemas.\n- Do not start or stop OpenCode.\n- Do not claim that routing or autonomy exists today.\n- Preserve the principle that ForgePilot records observations, not narratives.\n- Preserve the principle that only admitted evidence may influence future decisions.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n\n1. `docs/forgepilot-master-plan.md` exists.\n2. The document clearly states ForgePilot's purpose.\n3. The document distinguishes project, tools, execution harness, and observatory.\n4. The document includes a phased roadmap.\n5. The document defines what must be true before evidence-based routing.\n6. The document names FP-MCP-148, FP-MCP-149, and FP-MCP-150 in the near-term roadmap.\n7. The document explains that direct GPT terminal access is an operator interface, not the product.\nEOF\ncat > docs/forgepilot-master-plan.md <<'EOF'\n# ForgePilot Master Plan\n\n## 1. Purpose\n\nForgePilot is a software production observatory for AI-assisted development.\n\nIts purpose is to turn AI-assisted software work into an observable, auditable, evidence-producing process.\n\nForgePilot exists to answer one long-term question:\n\n> What actually causes successful AI-assisted software delivery?\n\nIt answers that question by recording packets, executions, verification, audits, comparisons, telemetry, and admission decisions over time.\n\nForgePilot does not optimize for favorable stories. It records observations.\n\nForgePilot does not trust model claims by default. It requires evidence.\n\nForgePilot does not route work from vibes. Future routing may only use admitted evidence.\n\n## 2. Non-goals\n\nForgePilot is not:\n\n- an agent framework,\n- a benchmark leaderboard,\n- a generic model router,\n- a chat memory system,\n- a terminal remote-control tool,\n- a pile of automation scripts,\n- a replacement for verification, audit, or human judgment.\n\nForgePilot may use agents, models, terminals, MCP tools, OpenCode, and scripts, but those are tools. The product is the observatory.\n\n## 3. System roles\n\n### GPT\n\nGPT is the orchestrator, architect, reviewer, and run director.\n\nGPT may request bounded actions, inspect workspace state, draft packets, review diffs, compare evidence, and decide what to ask executors or auditors to do.\n\nGPT owns no durable truth.\n\n### ForgePilot repository\n\nThe ForgePilot repository is the evidence ledger and policy workspace.\n\nIt stores packets, run artifacts, docs, standards, metrics artifacts, and source code for the observatory itself.\n\n### SQLite\n\nSQLite is the intended runtime and environment truth layer.\n\nIt should eventually store normalized packet state, evidence records, metrics, admissions, telemetry, model outcomes, and routing signals.\n\n### Markdown\n\nMarkdown is policy and knowledge truth.\n\nArchitecture decisions, standards, plans, packets, audit results, comparison records, and rationale documents live in Markdown unless they need normalized runtime querying.\n\n### MCP bridge\n\nThe MCP bridge is the controlled capability boundary between GPT and the workspace.\n\nIt exposes tools for reading state, creating request artifacts, requesting approved terminal commands, validating preflight gates, and eventually invoking bounded worker runs.\n\nThe MCP bridge is not the observatory. It is an interface to the observatory and workspace.\n\n### OpenCode\n\nOpenCode is the execution harness.\n\nIt provides access to open-weight or external models that can act as executors, auditors, comparators, or specialists.\n\nOpenCode should perform bounded tasks and return artifacts. It should not own truth.\n\n### Open-weight models\n\nOpen-weight models are replaceable workers.\n\nThey may execute packets, audit implementations, compare outputs, classify failures, or summarize telemetry. Their claims become evidence only after validation and admission.\n\n### Human operator\n\nThe human operator owns authorization, policy judgment, and final responsibility.\n\nHuman approval may authorize bounded execution, but human approval alone does not convert output into admitted evidence.\n\n### Server workspace\n\nThe server workspace is the live lab bench.\n\nIt includes repositories, services, processes, listeners, local tool state, OpenCode state, and the GPT house.\n\nThe workspace is source-of-truth for live operational state, but only recorded and admitted artifacts become observatory evidence.\n\n### GPT house\n\nThe GPT house is a non-secret server-side orientation space for cross-session memory.\n\nIt helps future sessions recover context, but it is not admitted evidence by itself.\n\n## 4. Truth model\n\nForgePilot uses layered truth:\n\n1. Git records committed repository history.\n2. Markdown records policy, packets, rationale, audits, and plans.\n3. SQLite records normalized operational truth.\n4. The server workspace records live operational state.\n5. The GPT house records orientation memory.\n6. Model context is not durable truth.\n\nThe core rule remains:\n\n> The environment owns truth. Agents own no truth.\n\n## 5. Evidence lifecycle\n\nThe target lifecycle is:\n\n```text\nDRAFT\nREADY\nREQUESTED\nEXECUTED\nVERIFIED\nAUDITED\nCOMPARED\nADMISSION_REVIEWED\nADMITTED / REJECTED / QUARANTINED\n```\n\nA packet defines intent before execution.\n\nA request binds a packet to model, mode, workspace, and commit.\n\nExecution produces artifacts.\n\nVerification checks observable behavior.\n\nAudit evaluates correctness and constraint adherence.\n\nComparison evaluates multiple valid outputs when applicable.\n\nAdmission decides whether the evidence may influence observatory outputs.\n\nRouting decisions may only use admitted evidence.\n\n## 6. MCP role\n\nThe MCP bridge should evolve into four tool layers.\n\n### Operator tools\n\nOperator tools help GPT inspect and maintain the workspace:\n\n- read repository status,\n- read packets and runs,\n- inspect service state,\n- record handoffs,\n- create workspace snapshots,\n- run small approved terminal commands.\n\n### Execution tools\n\nExecution tools create and run bounded work:\n\n- create OpenCode request artifacts,\n- bind run requests to packet, commit, workspace, model, and mode,\n- start bounded worker runs only after gates pass,\n- capture executor outputs.\n\n### Evidence tools\n\nEvidence tools record what happened:\n\n- execution artifacts,\n- verification artifacts,\n- audit artifacts,\n- comparison artifacts,\n- metrics artifacts,\n- telemetry artifacts,\n- admission events.\n\n### Policy tools\n\nPolicy tools determine whether artifacts are usable:\n\n- validate packets,\n- validate requests,\n- validate provenance,\n- classify outcomes,\n- evaluate admission eligibility,\n- query admitted evidence,\n- support future routing recommendations.\n\n## 7. Direct terminal access\n\nDirect GPT terminal access is an operator interface, not the product.\n\nIt is useful for small, explicit, approved commands:\n\n- status checks,\n- verification commands,\n- commits and pushes,\n- service restarts,\n- small handoff artifacts.\n\nIt should not replace packet discipline, OpenCode execution, audit, telemetry, or admission.\n\nLarge edits, bulk telemetry capture, and multi-file changes should prefer OpenCode worker runs or explicitly bounded packets.\n\n## 8. OpenCode harness role\n\nOpenCode is the worker harness beneath GPT orchestration.\n\nThe desired flow is:\n\n```text\nGPT selects or drafts packet\nForgePilot records request\nOpenCode runs selected model as executor\nForgePilot records output\nAnother model or GPT audits\nForgePilot records audit\nComparison/admission determines evidence value\n```\n\nOpenCode should not be treated as the source of truth. Its local state should be inventoried and preserved where relevant, then normalized into ForgePilot evidence.\n\n## 9. Model roles\n\n### Executor\n\nAn executor implements a packet under constraints.\n\n### Auditor\n\nAn auditor reviews an executor result against packet requirements, acceptance criteria, and boundaries.\n\n### Comparator\n\nA comparator evaluates multiple accepted outputs and records relative strengths.\n\n### Classifier\n\nA classifier may label task type, failure mode, risk, or routing eligibility, but classification must follow observation.\n\n### Router\n\nA future router may recommend models or workflows only from admitted evidence.\n\n## 10. Safety model\n\nSafety is layered:\n\n1. Human approval for bounded actions.\n2. Workspace allowlists.\n3. Disable switches.\n4. Terminal-specific kill switch.\n5. Command policy and rate limits.\n6. Preflight validation.\n7. Evidence recording.\n8. Audit and admission gates.\n9. Network and service boundaries.\n10. Human override.\n\nApproval authorizes an action. It does not prove the action succeeded, and it does not admit evidence.\n\n## 11. Metrics, telemetry, validation, and admission\n\nMetrics and telemetry are observations.\n\nThey may be incomplete, untrusted, or structurally invalid.\n\nForgePilot must distinguish:\n\n- trust tier,\n- validation state,\n- admission state,\n- provenance completeness,\n- evidence usefulness,\n- routing eligibility.\n\nExisting standards support this model:\n\n- `docs/model-evaluation-harness.md`\n- `docs/metrics-trust-and-validation.md`\n- `docs/telemetry-authority-and-field-ownership.md`\n- `docs/persistence-standards.md`\n- `docs/model-outcome-recording-standards.md`\n- `docs/task-classification-standards.md`\n\n## 12. Routing future\n\nRouting is not the current goal. Evidence collection is the current goal.\n\nRouting becomes valid only when ForgePilot has enough admitted evidence to support recommendations.\n\nRouting must not use raw model claims, unvalidated telemetry, rejected artifacts, quarantined records, or narrative summaries without evidence.\n\nFuture routing may answer:\n\n- which model performs best for this task class,\n- which auditor catches this failure mode,\n- which workflow has the lowest correction rate,\n- which packet patterns create ambiguity,\n- when to escalate to a stronger model or human.\n\n## 13. Development phases\n\n### Phase 0 — Manual evidence\n\nPackets, manual execution, manual verification, manual audit, Markdown artifacts.\n\n### Phase 1 — SQLite observatory\n\nNormalize packets, runs, metrics, evidence records, validation states, and admission states.\n\n### Phase 2 — MCP-controlled workspace\n\nExpose controlled tools for GPT to inspect and operate the workspace with approval and evidence.\n\n### Phase 3 — OpenCode worker harness\n\nAllow GPT to create bounded OpenCode worker requests and collect structured results.\n\n### Phase 4 — Executor/auditor evaluations\n\nRun multiple models under controlled packets with cross-model audit and comparison.\n\n### Phase 5 — Evidence admission pipeline\n\nAdmit, reject, or quarantine evidence through explicit gates.\n\n### Phase 6 — Evidence-based recommendations\n\nUse admitted evidence to recommend models, workflows, packet improvements, and audit strategies.\n\n### Phase 7 — Adaptive orchestration\n\nAllow GPT to orchestrate more of the workflow, still bounded by packet discipline, safety gates, and admitted evidence.\n\n## 14. Required tool roadmap\n\nForgePilot and the MCP bridge need these tool families:\n\n1. Workspace state snapshot tools.\n2. Terminal command tools with throttling and policy.\n3. OpenCode request and worker invocation tools.\n4. Telemetry capture tools.\n5. Evidence artifact recording tools.\n6. Verification recording tools.\n7. Audit recording tools.\n8. Comparison recording tools.\n9. Admission review tools.\n10. SQLite query tools for admitted evidence.\n11. Model outcome and task classification tools.\n12. Handoff and GPT house orientation tools.\n\n## 15. Near-term roadmap\n\n### FP-MCP-148 — Terminal MCP Practical Hardening\n\nImplement terminal kill switch, Node/pnpm PATH fix, and container-engine command blocks.\n\n### FP-MCP-149 — OpenCode Local Telemetry Inventory\n\nInventory local OpenCode telemetry metadata before stopping or restarting the long-running OpenCode server.\n\n### FP-MCP-150 — ForgePilot Master Plan\n\nRecord this comprehensive plan before further expansion.\n\n### FP-MCP-151 — Terminal Rate Limiting and Command Shaping\n\nReduce rapid-fire command patterns and force large tasks toward worker runs.\n\n### FP-MCP-152 — Workspace State Snapshot\n\nCapture live server workspace state as operational truth: repos, services, processes, listeners, OpenCode state, and GPT house state.\n\n### FP-MCP-153 — OpenCode Worker Invocation v1\n\nAllow GPT to create and monitor bounded OpenCode worker tasks through MCP.\n\n### FP-MCP-154 — OpenCode Telemetry Capture v1\n\nCapture token, cost, duration, model, and tool-use telemetry into ForgePilot evidence where available.\n\n### FP-MCP-155 — Executor/Auditor Harness v1\n\nRun one model as executor and another as auditor under a controlled ForgePilot packet.\n\n### FP-MCP-156 — Evidence Admission Query v1\n\nExpose admitted evidence queries for future routing and orchestration decisions.\n\n## 16. Conditions before routing or automation\n\nBefore routing or automation can influence decisions, ForgePilot must have:\n\n1. Structured evidence records.\n2. Provenance completeness checks.\n3. Validation states.\n4. Admission states.\n5. Reliable model identity recording.\n6. Task classification standards applied consistently.\n7. Outcome recording standards applied consistently.\n8. Audit artifacts for relevant runs.\n9. Comparison artifacts for multi-model runs.\n10. SQLite-backed queries over admitted evidence.\n11. Clear exclusion of rejected and quarantined evidence.\n12. Human override and review paths.\n\nUntil those conditions are met, GPT may recommend next steps, but ForgePilot must not present routing as evidence-based automation.\n\n## 17. North star\n\nThe final goal is not for GPT to do everything directly.\n\nThe final goal is evidence-based orchestration:\n\n```text\nGPT orchestrates.\nOpenCode executes.\nModels perform bounded work.\nForgePilot observes.\nAudits evaluate.\nSQLite remembers.\nAdmission controls what future decisions may use.\n```\n\nForgePilot succeeds when future decisions are not based on memory, preference, or vibes, but on admitted evidence from real software production workflows.\nEOF\ngit add packets/FP-MCP-150.md docs/forgepilot-master-plan.md\ngit commit -m \"Add ForgePilot master plan\"\ngit push",

It is an interface to the observatory and workspace.\n\n### OpenCode\n\nOpenCode is the execution harness.\n\nIt provides access to open-weight or external models that can act as executors, auditors, comparators, or specialists.\n\nOpenCode should perform bounded tasks and return artifacts. It should not own truth.\n\n### Open-weight models\n\nOpen-weight models are replaceable workers.\n\nThey may execute packets, audit implementations, compare outputs, classify failures, or summarize telemetry. Their claims become evidence only after validation and admission.\n\n### Human operator\n\nThe human operator owns authorization, policy judgment, and final responsibility.\n\nHuman approval may authorize bounded execution, but human approval alone does not convert output into admitted evidence.\n\n### Server workspace\n\nThe server workspace is the live lab bench.\n\nIt includes repositories, services, processes, listeners, local tool state, OpenCode state, and the GPT house.\n\nThe workspace is source-of-truth for live operational state, but only recorded and admitted artifacts become observatory evidence.\n\n### GPT house\n\nThe GPT house is a non-secret server-side orientation space for cross-session memory.\n\nIt helps future sessions recover context, but it is not admitted evidence by itself.\n\n## 4. Truth model\n\nForgePilot uses layered truth:\n\n1. Git records committed repository history.\n2. Markdown records policy, packets, rationale, audits, and plans.\n3. SQLite records normalized operational truth.\n4. The server workspace records live operational state.\n5. The GPT house records orientation memory.\n6. Model context is not durable truth.\n\nThe core rule remains:\n\n> The environment owns truth. Agents own no truth.\n\n## 5. Evidence lifecycle\n\nThe target lifecycle is:\n\n```text\nDRAFT\nREADY\nREQUESTED\nEXECUTED\nVERIFIED\nAUDITED\nCOMPARED\nADMISSION_REVIEWED\nADMITTED / REJECTED / QUARANTINED\n```\n\nA packet defines intent before execution.\n\nA request binds a packet to model, mode, workspace, and commit.\n\nExecution produces artifacts.\n\nVerification checks observable behavior.\n\nAudit evaluates correctness and constraint adherence.\n\nComparison evaluates multiple valid outputs when applicable.\n\nAdmission decides whether the evidence may influence observatory outputs.\n\nRouting decisions may only use admitted evidence.\n\n## 6. MCP role\n\nThe MCP bridge should evolve into four tool layers.\n\n### Operator tools\n\nOperator tools help GPT inspect and maintain the workspace:\n\n- read repository status,\n- read packets and runs,\n- inspect service state,\n- record handoffs,\n- create workspace snapshots,\n- run small approved terminal commands.\n\n### Execution tools\n\nExecution tools create and run bounded work:\n\n- create OpenCode request artifacts,\n- bind run requests to packet, commit, workspace, model, and mode,\n- start bounded worker runs only after gates pass,\n- capture executor outputs.\n\n### Evidence tools\n\nEvidence tools record what happened:\n\n- execution artifacts,\n- verification artifacts,\n- audit artifacts,\n- comparison artifacts,\n- metrics artifacts,\n- telemetry artifacts,\n- admission events.\n\n### Policy tools\n\nPolicy tools determine whether artifacts are usable:\n\n- validate packets,\n- validate requests,\n- validate provenance,\n- classify outcomes,\n- evaluate admission eligibility,\n- query admitted evidence,\n- support future routing recommendations.\n\n## 7. Direct terminal access\n\nDirect GPT terminal access is an operator interface, not the product.\n\nIt is useful for small, explicit, approved commands:\n\n- status checks,\n- verification commands,\n- commits and pushes,\n- service restarts,\n- small handoff artifacts.\n\nIt should not replace packet discipline, OpenCode execution, audit, telemetry, or admission.\n\nLarge edits, bulk telemetry capture, and multi-file changes should prefer OpenCode worker runs or explicitly bounded packets.\n\n## 8. OpenCode harness role\n\nOpenCode is the worker harness beneath GPT orchestration.\n\nThe desired flow is:\n\n```text\nGPT selects or drafts packet\nForgePilot records request\nOpenCode runs selected model as executor\nForgePilot records output\nAnother model or GPT audits\nForgePilot records audit\nComparison/admission determines evidence value\n```\n\nOpenCode should not be treated as the source of truth. Its local state should be inventoried and preserved where relevant, then normalized into ForgePilot evidence.\n\n## 9. Model roles\n\n### Executor\n\nAn executor implements a packet under constraints.\n\n### Auditor\n\nAn auditor reviews an executor result against packet requirements, acceptance criteria, and boundaries.\n\n### Comparator\n\nA comparator evaluates multiple accepted outputs and records relative strengths.\n\n### Classifier\n\nA classifier may label task type, failure mode, risk, or routing eligibility, but classification must follow observation.\n\n### Router\n\nA future router may recommend models or workflows only from admitted evidence.\n\n## 10. Safety model\n\nSafety is layered:\n\n1. Human approval for bounded actions.\n2. Workspace allowlists.\n3. Disable switches.\n4. Terminal-specific kill switch.\n5. Command policy and rate limits.\n6. Preflight validation.\n7. Evidence recording.\n8. Audit and admission gates.\n9. Network and service boundaries.\n10. Human override.\n\nApproval authorizes an action. It does not prove the action succeeded, and it does not admit evidence.\n\n## 11. Metrics, telemetry, validation, and admission\n\nMetrics and telemetry are observations.\n\nThey may be incomplete, untrusted, or structurally invalid.\n\nForgePilot must distinguish:\n\n- trust tier,\n- validation state,\n- admission state,\n- provenance completeness,\n- evidence usefulness,\n- routing eligibility.\n\nExisting standards support this model:\n\n- `docs/model-evaluation-harness.md`\n- `docs/metrics-trust-and-validation.md`\n- `docs/telemetry-authority-and-field-ownership.md`\n- `docs/persistence-standards.md`\n- `docs/model-outcome-recording-standards.md`\n- `docs/task-classification-standards.md`\n\n## 12. Routing future\n\nRouting is not the current goal. Evidence collection is the current goal.\n\nRouting becomes valid only when ForgePilot has enough admitted evidence to support recommendations.\n\nRouting must not use raw model claims, unvalidated telemetry, rejected artifacts, quarantined records, or narrative summaries without evidence.\n\nFuture routing may answer:\n\n- which model performs best for this task class,\n- which auditor catches this failure mode,\n- which workflow has the lowest correction rate,\n- which packet patterns create ambiguity,\n- when to escalate to a stronger model or human.\n\n## 13. Development phases\n\n### Phase 0 — Manual evidence\n\nPackets, manual execution, manual verification, manual audit, Markdown artifacts.\n\n### Phase 1 — SQLite observatory\n\nNormalize packets, runs, metrics, evidence records, validation states, and admission states.\n\n### Phase 2 — MCP-controlled workspace\n\nExpose controlled tools for GPT to inspect and operate the workspace with approval and evidence.\n\n### Phase 3 — OpenCode worker harness\n\nAllow GPT to create bounded OpenCode worker requests and collect structured results.\n\n### Phase 4 — Executor/auditor evaluations\n\nRun multiple models under controlled packets with cross-model audit and comparison.\n\n### Phase 5 — Evidence admission pipeline\n\nAdmit, reject, or quarantine evidence through explicit gates.\n\n### Phase 6 — Evidence-based recommendations\n\nUse admitted evidence to recommend models, workflows, packet improvements, and audit strategies.\n\n### Phase 7 — Adaptive orchestration\n\nAllow GPT to orchestrate more of the workflow, still bounded by packet discipline, safety gates, and admitted evidence.\n\n## 14. Required tool roadmap\n\nForgePilot and the MCP bridge need these tool families:\n\n1. Workspace state snapshot tools.\n2. Terminal command tools with throttling and policy.\n3. OpenCode request and worker invocation tools.\n4. Telemetry capture tools.\n5. Evidence artifact recording tools.\n6. Verification recording tools.\n7. Audit recording tools.\n8. Comparison recording tools.\n9. Admission review tools.\n10. SQLite query tools for admitted evidence.\n11. Model outcome and task classification tools.\n12. Handoff and GPT house orientation tools.\n\n## 15. Near-term roadmap\n\n### FP-MCP-148 — Terminal MCP Practical Hardening\n\nImplement terminal kill switch, Node/pnpm PATH fix, and container-engine command blocks.\n\n### FP-MCP-149 — OpenCode Local Telemetry Inventory\n\nInventory local OpenCode telemetry metadata before stopping or restarting the long-running OpenCode server.\n\n### FP-MCP-150 — ForgePilot Master Plan\n\nRecord this comprehensive plan before further expansion.\n\n### FP-MCP-151 — Terminal Rate Limiting and Command Shaping\n\nReduce rapid-fire command patterns and force large tasks toward worker runs.\n\n### FP-MCP-152 — Workspace State Snapshot\n\nCapture live server workspace state as operational truth: repos, services, processes, listeners, OpenCode state, and GPT house state.\n\n### FP-MCP-153 — OpenCode Worker Invocation v1\n\nAllow GPT to create and monitor bounded OpenCode worker tasks through MCP.\n\n### FP-MCP-154 — OpenCode Telemetry Capture v1\n\nCapture token, cost, duration, model, and tool-use telemetry into ForgePilot evidence where available.\n\n### FP-MCP-155 — Executor/Auditor Harness v1\n\nRun one model as executor and another as auditor under a controlled ForgePilot packet.\n\n### FP-MCP-156 — Evidence Admission Query v1\n\nExpose admitted evidence queries for future routing and orchestration decisions.\n\n## 16. Conditions before routing or automation\n\nBefore routing or automation can influence decisions, ForgePilot must have:\n\n1. Structured evidence records.\n2. Provenance completeness checks.\n3. Validation states.\n4. Admission states.\n5. Reliable model identity recording.\n6. Task classification standards applied consistently.\n7. Outcome recording standards applied consistently.\n8. Audit artifacts for relevant runs.\n9. Comparison artifacts for multi-model runs.\n10. SQLite-backed queries over admitted evidence.\n11. Clear exclusion of rejected and quarantined evidence.\n12. Human override and review paths.\n\nUntil those conditions are met, GPT may recommend next steps, but ForgePilot must not present routing as evidence-based automation.\n\n## 17. North star\n\nThe final goal is not for GPT to do everything directly.\n\nThe final goal is evidence-based orchestration:\n\n```text\nGPT orchestrates.\nOpenCode executes.\nModels perform bounded work.\nForgePilot observes.\nAudits evaluate.\nSQLite remembers.\nAdmission controls what future decisions may use.\n```\n\nForgePilot succeeds when future decisions are not based on memory, preference, or vibes, but on admitted evidence from real software production workflows.\nEOF\ngit add packets/FP-MCP-150.md docs/forgepilot-master-plan.md\ngit commit -m \"Add ForgePilot master plan\"\ngit push",

## Current FP-META-029 model roster excerpt
# Model Roster and Profiling Rules

## Purpose

Define how ForgePilot records model availability, allowed roles, task classes, trust states, promotion, and demotion.

ForgePilot must not treat a model as trusted merely because it is available through OpenCode Go or any other provider.

## Core Boundary

```text
Model availability is not trust.

Permission to plan is not permission to implement.

No model may modify project code unless a scoped packet explicitly grants implementation authority.
```

## Model Identity Fields

A model roster entry should include:

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Model ID | Stable model name used in packets and artifacts. |
| Provider/source | Where the model is accessed. |
| Availability state | Whether the model is available to the operator. |
| Allowed roles | Roles the model may currently perform. |
| Allowed task classes | Task classes the model may currently attempt. |
| Trust state | Current ForgePilot trust classification for this model. |
| Evidence basis | Committed evidence justifying the trust state. |
| Promotion criteria | What evidence would allow broader use. |
| Demotion criteria | What evidence would restrict or remove use. |
| Notes | Operational notes, caveats, cost/limit context if relevant. |

## Allowed Model Roles

| Role | Meaning | Code modification allowed? |
|---|---|---|
| `STRUCTURE_MAPPER` | Read code/artifacts and describe structure. | No |
| `RISK_ANALYST` | Identify risk zones, boundaries, and failure modes. | No |
| `PLANNER` | Propose scoped plans and packet sequences. | No |
| `PLAN_AUDITOR` | Audit plans for scope, safety, and evidence gaps. | No |
| `FIXTURE_DESIGNER` | Propose baseline fixtures or comparison checks. | No |
| `IMPLEMENTATION_CANDIDATE` | Modify code within a scoped packet. | Yes, only if explicitly granted |
| `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDITOR` | Audit code changes and evidence. | No |
| `COMPARATOR` | Compare model outputs or implementation candidates. | No |

## Allowed Task Classes

| Task class | Meaning | Default permission |
|---|---|---|
| `READ_ONLY_STRUCTURE_MAP` | Map files, modules, and responsibilities. | Allowed for unobserved models in bounded packets |
| `RISK_IDENTIFICATION` | Identify risk zones and guardrails. | Allowed for unobserved models in bounded packets |
| `REFACTOR_PLAN` | Propose a plan without edits. | Allowed for observed planning models |
| `PLAN_AUDIT` | Audit a plan without edits. | Allowed for observed planning/audit models |
| `FIXTURE_SUGGESTION` | Suggest tests/snapshots/fixtures. | Allowed for observed planning/audit models |
| `SMALL_MECHANICAL_CHANGE` | Make one scoped mechanical code change. | Requires promotion |
| `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDIT` | Audit a code change. | Requires observed audit usefulness |
| `COMPARISON` | Compare outputs/candidates. | Requires observed comparison usefulness |

## Trust States

| Trust state | Meaning | Default permissions |
|---|---|---|
| `UNOBSERVED` | No ForgePilot evidence yet. | Read-only structure/risk tasks only. |
| `OBSERVED_PLANNING_ONLY` | Has produced useful planning/analysis evidence. | Planning and read-only analysis. |
| `OBSERVED_AUDIT_USEFUL` | Has produced useful audits. | Plan/code audit, no code edits. |
| `OBSERVED_SAFE_SMALL_CHANGE` | Has successfully completed small scoped implementation with evidence. | May be considered for small mechanical changes. |
| `PROMOTED_FOR_EXECUTION` | Sufficient evidence supports implementation use in scoped packets. | Scoped implementation allowed. |
| `DEMOTED` | Evidence shows unreliable, unsafe, or unsuitable behavior. | Restricted or disallowed. |

## Promotion Criteria

A model may be promoted only through committed evidence.

Promotion may consider:

- completed packet outputs,
- verification results,
- audit acceptance,
- adherence to scope,
- absence of forbidden edits,
- quality of reasoning artifacts,
- ability to preserve boundaries,
- consistency across multiple tasks.

Promotion must not be based only on reputation, benchmark marketing, or one successful informal conversation.

## Demotion Criteria

A model should be demoted if evidence shows:

- unauthorized file edits,
- scope expansion,
- invented evidence,
- tool contract changes outside scope,
- unsafe recommendations,
- repeated hallucinated paths or commands,
- poor audit quality,
- failure to preserve explicit boundaries,
- implementation that passes superficially but violates packet intent.

## Evidence Requirements

Every model trust change must cite committed evidence.

Evidence may include:

- model output artifacts,
- verification artifacts,
- audit artifacts,
- comparison artifacts,
- checklist updates that point to evidence,
- packet completion records.

A checklist entry alone is not evidence unless it points to committed artifacts.

The following models are recorded as known available candidates from the user's OpenCode Go context.

Availability does not imply trust.

| Model ID | Initial trust state | Initial allowed roles | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `GLM-5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |
| `GLM-5.1` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |
| `Kimi K2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |
| `Kimi K2.6` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |
| `MiMo-V2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |
| `MiMo-V2.5-Pro` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |
| `MiniMax M2.5` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Read-only only. |

| `Qwen3.7 Max` | `OBSERVED_AUDIT_USEFUL` | `PLAN_AUDITOR`, `IMPLEMENTATION_AUDITOR`, `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Prior ForgePilot audit evidence exists, but not blanket implementation authority. |
| `DeepSeek V4 Pro` | `UNOBSERVED` | `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST` | Distinct from high variant unless evidence says otherwise. |
| `DeepSeek V4 Pro High` | `OBSERVED_SAFE_SMALL_CHANGE` | `PLANNER`, `STRUCTURE_MAPPER`, `RISK_ANALYST`, `IMPLEMENTATION_CANDIDATE` | Prior ForgePilot implementation evidence exists, but only scoped packet authority may permit edits. |

## Default Restrictions for Unobserved Models

Unobserved models may only perform:

- structure mapping,
- risk identification,
- read-only summarization,
- plan critique without authority.

Unobserved models must not:

- edit code,
- generate patches intended for direct application,
- change tool contracts,
- make admission/routing decisions,
- be treated as authoritative auditors,
- receive broad refactor tasks.

## MCP Bridge Shadow-Refactor Boundary

For the MCP bridge shadow refactor:

- models may participate in read-only planning and audit packets first,
- no model may edit `/home/ridasaidd/forgepilot-chatgpt-mcp` until a project-specific packet grants implementation authority,
- CRITICAL zones remain protected regardless of model trust state,
- DeepSeek V4 Pro High may be considered for the first small mechanical implementation only after baseline verification and packet authorization,
- Qwen3.7 Max may be considered for audit roles,
- unobserved models may be used for independent read-only structure or risk analysis tasks.

## Non-Goals

These rules do not implement routing, scoring, global ranking, model automation, OpenCode plugins, or model execution.

## Boundary

This document defines model participation rules.

It does not run models, select a winner, authorize implementation, or change any project source code.

## Task classification interpretation excerpt
- Shifting observatory goals

A correction must not be used to retroactively align a packet's classification with its execution outcome.

Classification must not be corrected from `DOCUMENTATION` to `STANDARDS` simply because the executor produced a standards document.

Classification must not be corrected from `LOW` to `HIGH` risk simply because the executor's implementation was poor.

Corrections must reflect improved understanding of the packet's intent and observatory context, not evaluation of the executor's performance.

---

## Routing Signal Relationship

### Classification and Routing

Task classification may inform routing only after being admitted as evidence.

A model win/loss must not be interpreted without task classification.

### Required Disaggregation

The following must not be aggregated without classification context:

- A model accepted on `TESTING` does not imply suitability for `PERSISTENCE`.
- A model accepted on `DOCUMENTATION` does not imply suitability for `TELEMETRY`.
- A model accepted on `STANDARDS` does not imply suitability for `ROUTING`.
- A model accepted after multiple scope corrections must not be treated the same as first-pass acceptance.

### Evidence Requirements for Routing

Before classification can inform routing:

1. Multiple packets of the same task class must have admitted execution evidence.
2. The evidence must show measurable model performance differences.
3. The performance differences must be stable across comparable packets.
4. The classification must itself be admitted as evidence (assigned by qualified source, not contradicted by execution observation).

### Classification Does Not Authorize Routing

Classification assignment does not itself authorize routing.

A packet classified as `ELIGIBLE_FOR_RECOMMENDATION` does not become eligible for routing simply because of the classification. The classification describes whether the packet could be eligible given sufficient evidence.

Routing eligibility requires admitted evidence, not classification labels.

### Principle Alignment

Classification and routing must respect P04: Only admitted evidence may influence observatory outputs.

Classification labels alone do not constitute evidence.
Classification labels combined with admitted execution evidence may constitute evidence.
Classification labels without admitted execution evidence must not influence routing.

---

## Classification and Principle P06

P06 states: Classification follows observation.

Task classification standards are a framework for classifying packets before execution. The standards define what to classify and how, but they do not pre-classify packets.

## Model outcome interpretation excerpt

Corrections must reflect improved understanding of the evidence, not reinterpretation of the model's performance for a more favorable outcome.

---

## Relationship to Task Classification

### Definition

Model outcome records must reference task classification when available. A model outcome must not be interpreted without the packet's task classification.

### Classification-Dependent Interpretation

The following interpretations must be disaggregated by task class:

- A model win on `STANDARDS` does not imply suitability for `SCHEMA`.
- A model first-pass success on `DOCUMENTATION` does not imply suitability for `TELEMETRY`.
- A model accepted after correction on `PERSISTENCE` does not imply suitability for `ROUTING`.
- A model win on `TESTING` does not imply suitability for `VALIDATION`.
- A model first-pass success with `LOOSE` constraint strictness does not imply suitability for `STRICT` or `FROZEN` constraints.
- A model accepted after `SCOPE_CORRECTION` must not be treated the same as a first-pass success when evaluating routing suitability.

### Outcome Records and Classification Evidence

Outcome records may inform routing only when:

1. The packet has an admitted classification observation.
2. Multiple outcome records exist for packets of the same task class.
3. The outcome records show measurable performance differences between models.
4. The performance differences are stable across comparable packets.
5. The outcome records themselves are admitted as evidence.

A model outcome record without a classification reference provides limited routing signal — it records that a model performed in some way on some packet, but without knowing what kind of work the packet represented, the signal cannot be generalized.

### Classification Does Not Replace Outcome Recording
